Said the elder: "O brother, tell me, with whom have you spoken, that you have not learned all that I said?"

The younger answered: "O brother, I speak with myself. Every day I place myself before the judgement of God, to give account of myself. And ever do I feel within myself one that excuses my faults."

Said the elder: "O brother, what faults have you, who art perfect?"

The younger answered: "O brother, say not so, for I stand between two great faults: the one is that I do not know myself to be the greatest of sinners; the other, that I do not desire to do more penance than any other man."

The elder answered: "Now, how shouldst you know thyself to be the greatest of sinners, if you are the most perfect?"

The younger answered: "The first word that my master said to me when I took the habit of a Pharisee was this: that I ought to consider the goodness of others and mine own iniquity; for if I should do so I should perceive myself to be the greatest of sinners."

Said the elder: "O brother, whose goodness or whose faults considerest you on these mountains, seeing there are no men here?"

The younger answered: "I ought to consider the obedience of the sun and the planets, for they serve their Creator better than I. But them I condemn: either because they give not light as I desire, or because their heat is too great, or because the ground is wetted too much or too little."

Whereupon, hearing this, the elder said: "Brother, where have you learned this doctrine? For I am now ninety years old, seventy-five years whereof I have been a Pharisee."

The younger answered: "O brother, you say this in humility, for you are a holy one of God. Yet I answer you that God our Creator looks not on time, but looks on the heart: wherefore David, being fifteen years old, younger than six other his brethren, was chosen king of Israel, and became a prophet of God our Lord."

## Chapter 151


"This man was a true Pharisee," said Jesus to his disciples; "and may it please God that we be able on the day of judgement to have him for our friend."

Then Jesus embarked on a ship, and the disciples were grieved that they had forgotten to bring bread. Jesus rebuked them, saying: "Beware of the leaven of the Pharisees of our day, for a little leaven mars a mass of meal."

Then said the disciples one to another: "Now what leaven have we, if we have not even any bread?"

Then said Jesus: "O men of little faith, have you then forgotten what God wrought in Nain, where there was no sign of corn? And how many ate and were satisfied with five loaves and two fishes? The leaven of the Pharisee is want of faith in God, and thought of self, which has corrupted not only the Pharisees of this time, but has corrupted Israel. For the simple folk, not knowing how to read, do that which they see the Pharisees do, because they hold them for holy ones.

"Know you what is the true Pharisee? He is the oil of human nature. For even as oil rests upon every liquor, so the goodness of the true Pharisee rests above all human goodness. He is a living book, which God gives to the world; for everything that he says and does is according to the law of God. Wherefore, whoso does as he does observes the law of God. The true Pharisee is salt that suffers not human flesh to putrefy by sin; for every one who sees him is brought to repentance. He is a light that lightens the pilgrims' way; for every one that considers his poverty with his penitence perceives that in this world we ought not to settle our heart.

"But he that makes the oil rancid, corrupts the book, putrefies the salt, and extinguishes the light — this man is a false Pharisee. If, therefore, you would not perish, beware that you do not as do the Pharisees of today."

## Chapter 152


Jesus having come to Jerusalem, and having entered one sabbath day into the Temple, the soldiers drew nigh to tempt him and take him, and they said: "Master, is it lawful to serve as a soldier?"

Jesus answered: "Our faith tells us that our life is a continual warfare upon the earth."

Said the soldiers: "So wouldst you convert us to your faith, and wouldst have us forsake the multitude of gods — for Rome alone has twenty-eight thousand gods that are seen — and follow your God, who is one only, and because he is not seen it is not known where he is, and perchance he is but vanity?"

Jesus answered: "If I had created you, as our God has created you, I would seek to convert you."

They answered: "Now how has your God created us, seeing it is not known where he is? Show us your God, and we will become Jews."

Then said Jesus: "If you had eyes to see him I would show him to you, but since you are blind I cannot show him to you."

The soldiers answered: "Of a surety, the honour which this people pays you must have taken away your understanding; for every one of us has two eyes in his head, and you say we are blind."

Jesus answered: "The carnal eyes cannot see save things gross and external; you therefore will be able to see nought but your gods of wood and silver and gold, that cannot do anything. But we of Judah have spiritual eyes, which are the fear and the faith of our God; wherefore we can see our God in every place."

The soldiers answered: "Beware how you speak; for if you pour contempt on our gods we will give you into the hand of Herod, who will take vengeance for our gods, who are omnipotent."

Jesus answered: "If they are omnipotent as you say, pardon me, for I will worship them."

The soldiers rejoiced at hearing this, and began to extol their idols.

Then said Jesus: "Herein is no need of words but of deeds; cause therefore that your gods create a fly, and I will worship them."

The soldiers were dismayed at hearing this, and knew not what to say. Wherefore Jesus said: "Assuredly, seeing they make not a single fly afresh, I will not for them forsake that God who has created everything with a single word; whose name alone affrights armies."

The soldiers answered: "Now show us this; for we are fain to take you." And they were fain to stretch forth their hands against Jesus.

Then said Jesus: "Adonai Sabaoth!" Whereupon straightway the soldiers were rolled out of the Temple, as one rolls casks of wood when they are washed to refill them with wine; insomuch that now their feet and now their head struck the ground, without any one touching them. And they were so affrighted, and fled in such wise, that they were never more seen in Judaea.

## Chapter 153


The priests and the Pharisees murmured among themselves, saying: "He has the wisdom of Baal and Ashtaroth — that is why he has done this by the power of Satan." Jesus opened his mouth and said: "Our God commanded that no one steal his neighbour's goods. And yet this one commandment alone is so violated and defiled that it has filled the world with sin — and with such sin as shall never be remitted the way other sins are remitted. For in every other sin, if a man grieves over it and does it no more, and fasts and prays and gives alms, our God, mighty and merciful, pardons him. But this sin is such that it shall never be remitted unless the wrongly taken thing is restored."

Then a scribe said: "Master, how has robbery filled the world with sin? Surely now, by God's grace, there are but few robbers — they can no sooner show their faces than the soldiery hangs them."

Jesus answered: "Whoever does not know goods cannot know the robbers. No — truly I tell you that many rob and do not know what they do, and so their sin is greater than the others'. For the unknown sickness is not healed."

Then the Pharisees drew near to Jesus and said: "Master, since you alone in Israel know the truth, teach us."

Jesus answered: "I do not say that I alone know the truth — for this word 'alone' belongs to God alone and to no one else, for He is the truth, who alone knows the truth. So if I said this, I would be a greater robber, for I would be stealing the honour of God. And by saying that I alone knew God, I would make myself fall into greater ignorance than all. So you committed a grave sin in saying that I alone know the truth. And I tell you that if you said this to tempt me, the sin is the greater."

Then Jesus said again, seeing that all were silent: "Though I am not the only one in Israel who knows the truth, I alone will speak. So listen to me, since you have questioned me. All created things belong to the Creator, so that nothing can claim anything as its own. Thus the soul, the senses, the flesh, time, goods, and honour — all are God's property. So if they are not received as God wills, one becomes a robber; and spending them likewise, contrary to what God wills, one is likewise a robber. So I tell you — as God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — when you take time, saying 'Tomorrow I will do this, I will say such a thing, I will go to such a place,' and do not say 'If God wills,' you are robbers. And you are greater robbers when you spend the best of your time on your own pleasure and not on God's pleasure, and spend the vilest of it in God's service — then you are robbers indeed. Whoever commits sin, of whatever kind it may be, is a robber — for he steals the time and the soul, with his life, which ought to serve God, and gives it to Satan, the enemy of God."

## Chapter 154


"The man, then, who has honour, life, and goods: when his property is stolen, the robber shall be hanged; when his life is taken, the murderer shall be beheaded — and this is just, for God has commanded it. But when a neighbour's honour is stolen, why is the robber not put upon the cross? Are goods, then, better than honour? Has God commanded, then, that he who steals goods shall be punished, and he who steals life together with goods shall be punished — but he who steals honour shall go free? Certainly not. For because of the murmuring, our fathers did not enter the land of promise — but their children did. And for this sin the serpents killed about seventy thousand of our people. As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands: he who steals honour deserves greater punishment than he who robs a man of goods and life. And he who listens to the murmurer is likewise guilty — for the one takes Satan upon his tongue, and the other into his ears."

The Pharisees burned with rage at hearing this, because they could not fault his speech. Then a doctor drew near to Jesus and said to him: "Good master, tell me: for what reason did God not grant corn and fruit to our fathers? He knew they were bound to fall — surely He should either have granted them the corn, or not have let man see it."

Jesus answered: "Man, you call me good — but you are wrong, for God alone is good. And you are much more wrong to ask why God has not acted according to your brain. But I will answer you fully all the same. So I tell you that God our Creator, in His working, does not shape Himself to us. Therefore it is not lawful for the creature to seek its own way and its own comfort, but rather the honour of God its Creator. So the creature depends on the Creator, and not the Creator on the creature. As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands: if God had granted man everything, man would never have known himself to be God's servant — he would have counted himself lord of paradise. Therefore the Creator, who is blessed forever, forbade him the food, so that man would remain subject to Him. And I tell you truly: he whose eyes' light is clear sees everything clearly, and draws light out of the very darkness — which the blind man does not. So I tell you: if man had not sinned, neither I nor you would know God's mercy and His justice. And if God had made man unable to sin, man would have been equal to God in that. Therefore blessed God created man good and upright, but free to do as he pleases concerning his own life — salvation or damnation."

The doctor was astonished at hearing this, and went away confounded.

## Chapter 155


Then the high priest called two old priests secretly and sent them to Jesus, who had gone out of the temple and was sitting in Solomon's porch, waiting to say the midday prayer. And near him he had his disciples with a great multitude of people. The priests drew near to Jesus and said: "Master, for what reason did man eat the corn and the fruit? Did God will him to eat it, or not?" And they said this to tempt him. For if he said "God willed it," they meant to answer, saying, "Why then did He forbid it?" And if he said "God did not will it," they meant to say: "Then man is stronger than God, since he works against God's will."

Jesus answered: "Your question is like the road over the mountain that has the cliff on the right hand and on the left. But I will walk in the middle." Hearing this, the priests were confounded, seeing that he knew their heart. Then Jesus said: "Every man, because he has need, works everything for his own gain. But God, who has no need of anything, worked according to His own good pleasure. So in creating man He created him free, so that man would know that God has no need of him. For instance, as a king does, who — to show his riches, and that his servants may love him more — gives freedom to his servants. So God created man free, that he might love his Creator much more and might know His generosity. For although God is almighty, having no need of man — having created him by His almightiness — He left him free out of His generosity, so that he could stand against evil and do good. For although God could have stopped sin, He would not go against His own generosity — for God does not contradict Himself. So — His almightiness and generosity having worked in man — He would not block man's sinning, I say — so that God's mercy and His justice might work in man. And as a sign that I speak the truth, I tell you: the high priest sent you to tempt me — and this is the fruit of his priesthood."

The old men left and reported everything to the high priest, who said: "This fellow has the devil on his back, who tells him everything. For he aspires to be king over Israel — but God will see to him."

## Chapter 156


When he had made the midday prayer, Jesus, coming out of the temple, found a man blind from his mother's womb. The disciples asked him, saying: "Master, who sinned in this man — his father or his mother — that he was born blind?"

Jesus answered: "Neither his father nor his mother sinned in him — but God created him so, as a testimony to the Gospel." And he called the blind man to him, spat on the ground, and made clay, and laid it upon the blind man's eyes, and said to him: "Go to the pool of Siloam and wash." The blind man went, and when he had washed he received light. And as he returned home, many who met him said: "If this man were blind, I would say for certain that he is the one who used to sit at the beautiful gate of the temple." Others said: "It is he — but how has he received his sight?" And they stopped him, saying: "Are you the blind man who used to sit at the beautiful gate of the temple?"

He answered: "I am he — and why do you ask?"

They said to him: "Then how did you get your sight?"

He answered: "A man made mud, spitting on the ground, and laid that mud upon my eyes, and said to me: 'Go and wash at the pool of Siloam.' I went and washed, and now I see. Blessed be the God of Israel!"

When the man born blind came back to the beautiful gate of the temple, all Jerusalem was filled with the news. So he was brought to the chief priest, who with the priests and Pharisees was plotting against Jesus. The high priest questioned him, saying: "Man, were you born blind?" "Yes," he answered. "Now give glory to God," said the high priest, "and tell us: what prophet appeared to you in a dream and gave you light? Was it our father Abraham, or Moses the servant of God, or some other prophet — for no others can do such a thing?"

The man born blind answered: "Neither Abraham nor Moses nor any prophet have I seen in a dream who healed me. But as I sat at the gate of the temple, a man made me come up to him, and made mud of earth with his spit. He put some of that mud upon my eyes and sent me to the pool of Siloam to wash. So I went and washed, and came back with the light of my eyes."

The high priest asked the name of such a man. The man born blind answered: "He did not tell me his name. But a man who saw this called me and said: 'Go and wash as that man told you — for he is Jesus the Nazarene, a prophet and a holy one of the God of Israel.'"

Then the high priest said: "Did he heal you today, by chance — for it is the Sabbath?" The blind man answered: "Today he healed me." The high priest said: "Now see what a sinner this fellow is, who does not keep the Sabbath."

## Chapter 157


The man born blind answered: "Whether he is a sinner, I do not know — but this I know: I was blind, and he has given me light."

The Pharisees did not believe this, so they said to the high priest: "Send for his father and mother — they will tell us the truth." So they sent for the father and mother of the blind man, and when they came the high priest questioned them, saying: "Is this man your son?" They answered: "He is truly our son." Then the high priest said: "He says he was born blind, and now he sees — how did this happen?" The father and mother of the man born blind answered: "He was truly born blind — but how he received his sight we do not know. He is of age; ask him, and he will tell you the truth." So they were dismissed, and the high priest said again to the man born blind: "Give glory to God and speak the truth."

The father and mother of the blind man were afraid to speak, for a decree had come from the Roman senate that no one should stand up for Jesus, the prophet of the Jews, on pain of death — this the governor had got passed. That is why they said: "He is of age; ask him."

The high priest said then to the man born blind: "Give glory to God and speak the truth — for we know that this man you say healed you is a sinner." The man born blind answered: "Whether he is a sinner I do not know — but this I know: I did not see, and he has given me light. It is certain that from the beginning of the world until this hour, no man born blind has ever been given light. And God does not listen to sinners."

The Pharisees said: "What then did he do when he gave you light?" The man born blind marvelled then at their unbelief and said: "I have told you already — why do you question me again? Do you also want to become his disciples?"

Then the high priest cursed him, saying: "You were born altogether in sin, and would you teach us? Go — you become a disciple of such a man! For we are disciples of Moses, and we know that God spoke to Moses — but this fellow, we do not know where he comes from." And they cast him out of the synagogue and the temple, forbidding him to pray with the clean of Israel.

## Chapter 158


The man born blind went to find Jesus, who comforted him, saying: "In no time have you been so blessed as you are now, for you are blessed of our God, who spake through David our father and his prophet against the friends of the world, saying: 'They curse and I bless'; and by Micah the prophet he said: 'I curse your blessing.' For earth is not so contrary to air, water to fire, light to darkness, heat to cold, or love to hate, as the will of God is contrary to the will of the world."

The disciples accordingly asked him, saying: "Lord, great are your words; tell us, therefore, the meaning, for as yet we understand them not."

Jesus answered: "When you shall know the world, you shall see that I have spoken the truth, and so shall you know the truth in every prophet. Know you, then, that three kinds of worlds are comprehended in a single name. The one is the heavens, with the earth, water, air and fire, with all the things that are inferior to man. Now this world in all things follows the will of God, for, as says David, prophet of God: 'God has given them a precept which they transgress not.'

"The second is all men, even as the house of such an one is called not for the walls, but for the family. Now this world, again, loves God, because by nature they long after God; insomuch that according to nature every one longs after God, even though they err in seeking God. And know you wherefore all long after God? Because they long every one after an infinite good without any evil, and this is God alone. Therefore the merciful God has sent his prophets to this world for its salvation.

"The third world is the evil constitution of men in sinning, which has transformed itself into a law contrary to God, the creator of the world — which makes man become like to the demons, God's enemies.

"And this world our God hates so, that if the prophets had loved this world — what think you! — assuredly God would have taken from them their prophecy. And what shall I say! As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands: when the messenger of God shall come to the world, if he should conceive love towards this wretched world, assuredly God would take away from him all that he gave him when he created him, and would make him reprobate — so greatly is God contrary to this world."

## Chapter 159


The disciples answered: "O master, exceeding great are your words; therefore have mercy upon us, for we understand them not."

Said Jesus: "Think you perchance that God has created his messenger to be his rival, who should be fain to make himself equal with God? Assuredly not, but rather as his good servant, who should not will that which his Lord wills not. You are not able to understand this because you know not what sin is. Wherefore hearken to my words. Truly, truly, I say to you, sin cannot arise in man save as a contradiction of God, seeing that that only is sin which God wills not; insomuch that all that God wills is most alien from sin.

"Accordingly, if our high priests and priests, with the Pharisees, persecuted me because the people of Israel has called me God, they would be doing a thing pleasing to God, and God would reward them. But because they persecute me for a contrary reason — since they will not have me say the truth, how they have contaminated the book of Moses and that of David, prophets and friends of God, by their traditions — and therefore hate me and desire my death, therefore God has them in abomination.

"Tell me — Moses slew men and Ahab slew men — is this in each case murder? Assuredly not. For Moses slew the men to destroy idolatry and to preserve the worship of the true God, but Ahab slew the men to destroy the worship of the true God and to preserve idolatry. Wherefore to Moses the slaying of men was converted into sacrifice, while to Ahab it was converted into sacrilege — insomuch that one and the same work produced these two contrary effects.

"As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands: if Satan had spoken to the angels in order to see how they loved God, he would not have been rejected of God. But because he sought to turn them away from God, therefore is he reprobate."

Then answered he who writes: "How, then, is to be understood that which was said in Micaiah the prophet, concerning the lie which God ordained to be spoken by the mouth of false prophets, as is written in the book of the kings of Israel?"

Jesus answered: "O Barnabas, recite briefly all that befell, that we may see the truth clearly."

## Chapter 160


Then said he who writes: "Daniel the prophet, describing the history of the kings of Israel and their tyrants, writes thus: 'The king of Israel joined himself with the king of Judah to fight against the sons of Belial — that is, reprobates — who were the Ammonites.

"'Now Jehoshaphat, king of Judah, and Ahab, king of Israel, being seated both on a throne in Samaria, there stood before them four hundred false prophets, who said to the king of Israel: "Go up against the Ammonites, for God will give them into your hands, and you shall scatter Ammon."

"'Then said Jehoshaphat: "Is there here any prophet of the God of our fathers?"

"'Ahab answered: "There is one only, and he is evil, for he always predicts evil concerning me; and him I hold in prison." And this he said — "there is only one" — because as many as were found had been slain by decree of Ahab, so that the prophets, even as you have said, O Master, were fled to the mountain tops where men dwelt not.

"'Then said Jehoshaphat: "Send for him here, and let us see what he says." Ahab therefore commanded that Micaiah be brought thither — who came with fetters on his feet, bewildered in countenance, like a man that dwells between life and death.

"'Ahab questioned him, saying: "Speak, Micaiah, in the name of God: shall we go up against the Ammonites? Will God give their cities into our hands?"

"'Micaiah answered: "Go up, go up, for prosperously shall you go up, and still more prosperously come down!"

"'Then the false prophets praised Micaiah as a true prophet of God, and loosed the fetters from his feet.

"'Jehoshaphat, who feared our God and had never bowed his knees before the idols, questioned Micaiah, saying: "For the love of the God of our fathers, speak the truth, as you have seen the issue of this war."

"'Micaiah answered: "O Jehoshaphat, I fear your face, wherefore I tell you that I have seen the people of Israel as sheep without a shepherd."

"'Then Ahab, laughing, said to Jehoshaphat: "I told you that this fellow predicts naught but evil, but you didst not believe it."

"'Then said they both: "Now how know you this, O Micaiah?"

"'Micaiah answered: "I heard a council of angels assembling in the presence of God, and I heard God say thus: 'Who will deceive Ahab, that he may go up against Ammon and be slain?' Whereupon one said one thing and another said another. Then came an angel and said: 'Lord, I will fight against Ahab, and will go to his false prophets and will put the lie into their mouth, and so shall he go up and be slain.' And hearing this, God said: 'Now go and do so, for you shall prevail.'"

"'Then were the false prophets enraged, and their chief smote Micaiah's cheek, saying: "O reprobate of God, when did the angel of truth depart from us and come to you? Tell us — when came to us the angel that brought the lie?"

"'Micaiah answered: "You shall know when you shall flee from house to house for fear of being slain, having deceived your king."

"'Then Ahab was wroth, and said: "Seize Micaiah, and the fetters which he had upon his feet place upon his neck, and keep him on barley bread and water until my return, for as yet I know not what death to give him."

"'They went up, then, and according to the word of Micaiah the matter befell. For the king of the Ammonites said to his servants: "See that you fight not against the king of Judah, nor against the princes of Israel, but slay the king of Israel, Ahab, mine enemy." ' "

Then said Jesus: "Stop there, Barnabas, for it is enough for our purpose."

## Chapter 161


"Have you heard all?" said Jesus. The disciples answered: "Yea, Lord."

Whereupon Jesus said: "Lying is indeed a sin, but murder is greater. For the lie is a sin that appertaineth to him that speaks it, but the murder, while it appertaineth to him that commits it, is such that it destroys the dearest thing that God has here upon earth — that is, man. And lying can be remedied by saying the contrary of that which has been said; whereas murder has no remedy, seeing it is not possible to give life again to the dead.

"Tell me, then: did Moses, the servant of God, sin in slaying all whom he slew?"

The disciples answered: "God forbid, God forbid that Moses should have sinned in obeying God who commanded him!"

Then said Jesus: "And I say, God forbid that that angel should have sinned who deceived Ahab's false prophets with the lie. For even as God received the slaughter of men as sacrifice, so received he the lie for praise. Truly, truly, I say to you, that even as the child errs which causes its shoes to be made by the measure of a giant, even so errs he who would subject God to the law, as he himself, as man, is subject to the law. When, therefore, you shall believe that only to be sin which God wills not, you will find the truth, even as I have told you.

"Wherefore, because God is not composite nor changeable, so also is he unable to will and not will a single thing; for so would he have contradiction in himself, and consequently pain, and would not be infinitely blessed."

Philip answered: "But how is that saying of the prophet Amos to be understood, that 'there is not evil in the city that God has not done'?"

Jesus answered: "Now here see, Philip, how great is the danger of resting in the letter, as do the Pharisees, who have invented for themselves the predestination of God in the elect, in such wise that they come to say in fact that God is unjust, a deceiver and a liar — and a fearful judgment that shall be upon them. Wherefore I say to you that here Amos, the prophet of God, speaks of the evil which the world calls evil; for if he had used the language of the righteous he would not have been understood of the world.

"For all tribulations are good: either they purge the evil that we have done, or they are good because they restrain us from doing evil, or they are good because they make man know the condition of this life, in order that we may love and long for life eternal.

"If, then, the prophet Amos had said: 'There is no good in the city but what God has wrought it,' he had given occasion for despair to the afflicted, as they beheld themselves in tribulation and sinners living in prosperity; and, what is worse, many, believing Satan to have such sovereignty over men, would have feared Satan and done him service, so as not to suffer tribulation.

"Amos therefore did as does the Roman interpreter, who considers not his words as one speaking in the presence of the high-priest, but considers the will and the business of the Jew that knows not to speak the Hebrew tongue."

## Chapter 162


"If Amos had said: 'There is no good in the city but what God has done it' — as God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — he would have made a grievous error. For the world holds not for good ought save the iniquities and sins that are done in the way of vanity. Whereupon men would have wrought much more iniquitously, believing that there be not any sin or wickedness which God has not done — at hearing whereof the earth trembles."

And when Jesus had said this, straightway there came a great earthquake, in so much that every one fell as dead. Jesus raised them up, saying: "Now see if I tell you the truth. But let this suffice you, then: that Amos, saying 'God has wrought evil in the city,' speaking with the world, spake of the tribulations, which only sinners call evil.

"Let us come now to the predestination, which you desire to know, of which I will speak to you beyond Jordan, the day after to-morrow, God willing."

## Chapter 163


Jesus went into the wilderness beyond the Jordan with his disciples, and when the midday prayer was done he sat down near a palm tree, and in the shade of the palm tree his disciples sat down. Then Jesus said: "So secret is predestination, O brothers, that in truth I tell you, only to one man shall it be known clearly — he who is the one the nations await, to whom the secrets of God are so clear that, when he comes into the world, blessed shall they be who hear his words, for God shall send his mercy upon them as this palm tree is over us. So as this tree shelters us from the burning of the sun, so shall the mercy of God shelter from Satan those who believe in that man."

The disciples answered: "O master, who shall that man be of whom you speak, who shall come into the world?"

Jesus answered with joy of heart: "He is Mohammed, messenger of God — who, when he comes into the world, even as the rain makes the earth fruitful when for a long time it has not rained, so shall he be a cause of good works among men through the abundant mercy that he shall bring. For he is a white cloud full of the mercy of God, which mercy God shall scatter upon the faithful like rain."

## Chapter 164


"I will tell you now, then, that little knowledge which God has granted me of this predestination. The Pharisees say that everything is so predestined that he who is elect cannot become reprobate, and he who is reprobate can by no means become elect; and that even as God has predestined good as the road by which the elect shall walk to salvation, so God has predestined sin as the road by which the reprobate shall walk to damnation. Cursed be the tongue that said this, with the hand that wrote it — for this is the faith of Satan. Wherefore who the Pharisees of the present time are may be known: they are faithful servants of Satan.

"What can predestination mean but an absolute will to bring a thing to its end, whereof one has the means in hand? For without the means one cannot destine an end. Now how shall he destine the house who not only has no stones and no money to spend, but has not even land to set one foot upon? Surely none. So then, I tell you, predestination — taking away the free will that God has given to man of his pure bounty — is not the law of God. Of a surety, what we shall be establishing is not predestination but abomination.

"That man is free the book of Moses shows, where, when our God gave the law upon Mount Sinai, he said thus: 'My commandment is not in heaven, that you should excuse yourself saying: Now who shall go to bring us the commandment of God, and who shall give us strength to observe it? Nor is it beyond the sea, that you should likewise excuse yourself. But my commandment is near your heart, that when you will you may observe it.'

"Tell me: if King Herod commanded an old man to become young and a sick man to become whole, and when they did it not caused them to be killed — would this be just?" The disciples answered: "It would be most unjust and impious for Herod to command this." Then Jesus, sighing, said: "These are the fruits of human traditions, O brothers — for in saying that God has predestined the reprobate so that he cannot become elect, they blaspheme God as impious and unjust. For he commands the sinner not to sin, and when he sins to repent — while such predestination takes away from the sinner the power not to sin, and deprives him altogether of repentance."

## Chapter 165


"But hear what God says by Joel the prophet: 'As your God lives — I will not the death of the sinner, but I seek that he be converted to repentance.' Will God then predestine that which he does not will? Consider what God says, and what the present Pharisees say. Further, God says by Isaiah the prophet: 'I have called, and you would not hear me.' And how much God has called, hear how he says by the same prophet: 'All the day I have spread out my hands to a people that believes me not, but contradicts me.' And our Pharisees, in saying that the reprobate cannot become elect, what do they say but that God mocks men — as one would mock a blind man by showing him white, and would mock a deaf man by speaking to his ears?

"And that the elect can become reprobate, consider what our God says by Ezekiel the prophet: 'As I live, says God — if the righteous shall forsake his righteousness so that he does abominations, he shall perish, and I will no more remember any of his righteousness; for trusting in it, it shall forsake him before me and shall not save him.' And of the calling of the reprobate, what does God say by Hosea the prophet but this: 'I will call the people not elect — I will call them elect.' God is true and cannot tell a lie, for being truth he speaks truth. But the present Pharisees with their doctrine contradict God altogether."

## Chapter 166


Andrew answered: "But how is it to be understood, what God said to Moses — that he will have mercy on whom he will have mercy, and will harden whom he will harden?"

Jesus answered: "God says this so that man may not believe that he is saved by his own virtue, but may know that life and the mercy of God have been given him by God of his bounty. And he says it so that the opinion of others as gods besides him may be shunned. So if he hardened Pharaoh, he did it because he had scourged our people and sought to scatter them by drowning all the male children of Israel — whereby Moses was near to losing his life.

"I tell you then in truth, that predestination has for its foundation the law of God with human free will — so much so that, though God could save the whole world with none perishing, he would not do it, so as not to deprive man of liberty; which he keeps for him to spite Satan, so that that clay scorned by the spirit, though it shall sin as the spirit did, may repent and go dwell where the spirit was cast out.

"Our God wills, I say, to follow man's free will with his mercy, and wills not with his omnipotence to abandon the creature. So on the day of judgment none will be able to make any excuse for their sins, seeing that then it will be manifest to them how much God has done for their conversion, and how often he has called them to repentance."

## Chapter 167


"If then your understanding will not rest content with this, and you would say again, 'Why so?' — I will open to you a 'why,' and it is this. Tell me: why can a stone not stay upon the water, yet the whole earth stays upon the water? Tell me: why does water quench fire, and earth flee from air — so that earth, water, air, and fire none can join together in peace — yet in man they are united and peacefully preserved?

"If then you know not this — nay, all men as men cannot know it — how shall they know that God created all things out of nothing with a single word? How shall they know the eternity of God? Surely they will still less be able to know this, for man being finite and composite with the body — which, as Solomon the prophet says, being corruptible, weighs down the soul — and the works of God being proportionate to God, how shall they be able to comprehend them?

"Isaiah, prophet of God, seeing this, cried out saying: 'Truly you are a hidden God!' And of the messenger of God — how God created him — he says: 'His generation, who shall narrate it?' And of the working of God he says: 'Who has been his counsellor?' So God said to human nature: 'As the heaven is exalted above the earth, so are my ways exalted above your ways, and my thoughts above your thoughts.'

"I tell you then that the manner of predestination is not manifest to men, although the fact is true, as I have told you. Ought man then, because he cannot find out the manner, to deny the fact? Surely I have never yet seen any one refuse health, though the manner be not understood — how God by my touch heals the sick, which is still unknown to me."

## Chapter 168


Then the disciples said: "Truly God speaks in you — for no man ever spoke like you."

Jesus answered: "Believe me: when God chose me to send me to the house of Israel, He gave me a book like a clear mirror, which came down into my heart — so that everything I speak comes forth from that book. And when that book has finished coming forth from my mouth, I shall be taken up from the world."

Peter answered: "Master — is even what you are saying now written in that book?"

Jesus answered: "All that I say for the knowledge of God and the service of God, for the knowledge of man and the salvation of man — all of it comes forth from that book, which is my gospel."

Peter said: "Is the glory of paradise written there?"

## Chapter 169


Jesus answered: "Listen, and I will tell you what paradise is like, and how the holy and the faithful shall live there without end — for this is one of the greatest blessings of paradise. Since everything, however great it is, having an end becomes small, yea nothing.

"Paradise is the house where God keeps His delights — and they are so great that the ground the feet of the saints and blessed shall tread is so precious that one drachm of it is more precious than a thousand worlds.

"Our father David, prophet of God, saw these delights — for God willed to show them to him, letting him see the glory of paradise. So when he came back to himself, he closed his eyes with both his hands and said, weeping: 'Look no more upon this world, O my eyes — for all is vain, with no good in it.'

"Of these delights Isaiah the prophet said: 'Eye of man has not seen, ear has not heard, nor has human heart understood, what God has prepared for those who love Him.'

"Do you know why they have not seen, heard, nor understood such delights? It is because, living here below, they are not worthy to see them. So although our father David truly saw them, I tell you in truth he did not see them with human eyes — for God drew his soul up to Himself, and thus, joined to God, he saw them by divine light.

"As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — the delights of paradise are infinite and man is finite, so man cannot take them in — just as a little earthen jar cannot hold the sea.

"See, then, how beautiful the world is in summertime, when everything brings forth fruit — so that the peasant, drunk with joy over the harvest at hand, makes valleys and mountains ring with his singing, loving his work with all his heart. In the same way, lift up your heart to paradise — where everything is fruitful, with fruits fitted to the one who tilled the ground.

"As God lives, this is enough for the knowledge of paradise — for God made paradise to be the house of His delights. Do you think immeasurable goodness would not have immeasurably good things? That immeasurable beauty would not have immeasurably beautiful things? Take heed — you would be greatly mistaken to think He did not."

## Chapter 170


"God says this to the man who shall serve Him faithfully: 'I know your works, that you work for Me. As I live for ever — your love shall never outrun My generosity. For you serve Me as God your Creator, knowing yourself to be My handiwork, and you ask of Me nothing but grace and mercy to serve Me faithfully. And because you put no end to My service, desiring to serve Me for ever — so will I do: I will reward you as if you were God, My equal. For I will not only put the abundance of paradise into your hands, but I will give you My very self as a gift. As you will be My servant for ever, so I will be your wages for ever.'"

## Chapter 171


"What do you think," Jesus said to his disciples, "of paradise? Is there a mind that can take in such riches and delights? A man would need to have as much knowledge as God has, if he wanted to know how much God will give to His servants.

"Have you seen how Herod gives a present to one of his favourite barons — the way he presents it?"

John answered: "I have seen it twice — and surely a tenth of what he gives would be enough for a poor man."

Jesus said: "But if a poor man were brought before Herod, what would he give him?"

John answered: "One or two mites."

"Now let this be your book to study, to know paradise by. For what God has given to man in this world for the body is like Herod giving one mite to a poor man. But what God will give to soul and body in paradise is like Herod giving all he has — his very life — to one of his servants."

## Chapter 172


"God says this to him who loves Him and serves Him faithfully: 'Go and consider, O My servant, the sand of the sea — see how much it is. If the sea gave you a single grain of sand, would it seem small to you? Surely, yes. As I your Creator live — all that I have given in this world to all the princes and kings of the earth is less than that grain of sand the sea would give you, compared to what I will give you in My paradise.'"

## Chapter 173


Then Jesus said: "Consider the abundance of paradise. For if God has given man in this world an ounce of good, in paradise he will give him ten hundred thousand loads. Consider the quantity of fruits that are in this world, the quantity of foods, the quantity of flowers, and the quantity of things that serve man. As God lives — in whose presence my soul stands — as the sea still has sand left when one takes a grain from it, so the quality and quantity of figs surpasses the sort of figs we eat here. And so with every other thing in paradise. But more than this I tell you in truth: as a mountain of gold and pearls is more precious than the shadow of an ant, so are the delights of paradise more precious than all the delights of the princes of the world, which they have had and shall have until the judgment of God, when the world shall have an end."

Peter answered: "Shall our body, then, which we now have, go into paradise?"

Jesus answered: "Beware, Peter, lest you become a Sadducee — for the Sadducees say that the flesh shall not rise again, and that there are no angels. So they are deprived of their soul and body going into paradise, and they are deprived of having any service from the angels in this world. Have you perhaps forgotten Job, prophet and friend of God, who says: 'I know that my God lives, and in the last day I shall rise again in my flesh, and with my eyes I shall see God my saviour'? But believe me: this flesh of ours shall be so purified that it shall have none of the properties that it has now — for it shall be purged of every evil desire, and God shall bring it back to the state that Adam was in before he sinned.

"Two servants serve one master in one same work. The one only sees the work and commands the second, and the second works as much as the first commands. Does it seem just to you, I say, that the master should reward only the one who sees and commands, and drive out of the house the one who laboured in the work? Surely not. Now how shall the justice of God bear it, that — the soul and the body serving God with the sense of man — the soul alone should see and command the service? For the soul, eating no bread, does not fast; it does not walk; it does not feel cold and heat; it does not fall sick, nor is it slain — for the soul is immortal, which suffers none of these bodily pains that the body suffers by way of the elements. Is it just, I say, that she alone should go into paradise, and not the body that laboured so much serving God?"

Peter answered: "O master, the body, having made the soul sin, ought not to be placed in paradise."

Jesus answered: "Now how shall the body sin without the soul? Assuredly it is impossible. So in taking away God's mercy from the body, you condemn the soul to hell."

## Chapter 174


"As God lives — in whose presence my soul stands — our God promises his mercy to the sinner, saying: 'In the hour when the sinner grieves over his sin, by myself I will not remember his iniquities forever.'

"Now who would eat the foods of paradise if the body did not go there? The soul? Surely not, seeing that she is spirit."

Peter answered: "Then the blessed shall eat in paradise — but how shall the food come out without uncleanness?"

Jesus answered [uncertain: printed "Bissposse iessu," OCR for "Rissposse" — read with the check]: "Now what blessedness shall the body have, if it neither eats nor drinks? Assuredly it is fitting to give glory in proportion to the one glorified. But you err, Peter, in thinking that such food shall come out in uncleanness — for this body at present eats corruptible foods, and so putrefaction comes forth. But in paradise the body shall be incorruptible, impassible, and immortal, free from every misery; and the foods, which are without any defect, shall generate no putrefaction at all."

## Chapter 175


God says thus in Isaiah the prophet, scorning the reprobate: "My servants shall sit at table in my house, and they shall feast joyfully with festivity and sounds of harps and organs, and I will let them lack nothing. But you who are my enemies shall be driven away from me, where you shall die of misery, while every servant of mine despises you."

## Chapter 176


"Of what use is it to say, 'They shall feast'?" Jesus said to his disciples. "Assuredly, God speaks plainly. But of what use are four rivers of precious liquor in paradise, with so many fruits? Assuredly, God does not eat; the angels do not eat; the soul does not eat; the sense does not eat — but the flesh eats, which is our body. So the glory of paradise belongs to the body in the matter of foods, and to the soul and sense in the matter of God and the company of angels and blessed spirits. Which glory shall be better made manifest by the messenger of God [uncertain: the printed column reads "dallo nontio di mo," the trailing "a" being the editors' gloss-letter; the check reads "the messenger of God"] — who, God having created all things for love of him, knows all things better than every other creature."

Bartholomew said: "O master, shall the glory of paradise be equal for every man? If it be equal, it shall not be just; and if it be not equal, the lesser shall envy the greater."

Jesus answered: "It shall not be equal, for God is just, and everyone shall be content, because there is no envy there. Tell me, Bartholomew: there is a master who has many servants, and he clothes all his servants in one same cloth. Do the boys, then, who wear boys' clothes, grieve that they do not have the garments of grown men? Surely, quite the opposite! If the grown ones wanted to put their bigger garments on them, they would grow angry — for the garments not being their size, they would think they were being mocked. Now, Bartholomew, lift your heart to God in paradise, and you shall see that all one glory — though it shall be more to one and less to another — shall bring them no envy at all."

## Chapter 177


Then he who writes said: "O master, does paradise have sunlight as this world does?"

Jesus answered: "God has said thus to me, O Barnabas: 'The world where you sinful men live has the sun and the moon and the stars that adorn it, for your benefit and gladness — I created it for this. So do you think, then, that the house where my faithful shall dwell would be no better? Assuredly you err in thinking this: for I, your God, am the sun of paradise, and my messenger is the moon, who receives all from me. And the stars are my prophets, who have preached my will to you. So my faithful — as my prophets brought them my word — so in the paradise of my delights they shall receive, for their delight and gladness.'"

## Chapter 178


"And let this suffice you," said Jesus, "for knowing paradise." Then Bartholomew said again: "Master, have patience with me if I ask you one word." Jesus answered: "Say what you desire." Bartholomew said: "Paradise is certainly great, for since such great goods are in it, it must needs be great." Jesus answered: "Paradise is so great that no man can measure it. I tell you in truth that the heavens are nine, among which stand the planets, which are distant one from another five hundred years of human journey; and the earth in like manner is distant from the first heaven five hundred years of journey. But stop at measuring the first heaven, which is as much greater than the whole earth as the whole earth is greater than a grain of sand. And so the second heaven is greater than the first, and the third than the second, and so each one, up to the last heaven, are greater, one than the other. Wherefore in truth I tell you that paradise is greater than all the earth and all the heavens, as the whole earth is greater than a grain of sand." Then Peter said: "Master, paradise must be greater than God, for God is seen inside it." Jesus answered: "Hold your peace, Peter, for you blaspheme and do not realize it."

## Chapter 179


Then the angel Gabriel came to Jesus, and showed him a mirror shining like the sun, in which he saw written these words: "As I live eternally, even as paradise is greater than all the heavens and the earth, as the whole earth is greater than a grain of sand, so am I greater than paradise, and as many times more as the sea has sand, as there are drops of water upon the sea, as there are blades of grass upon the earth, as there are leaves upon the trees, as there are skins upon the beasts — and as many times more as the grains of sand that would fill all the heavens and paradise and more." Then Jesus said: "Let us do reverence to our God, who is blessed eternally." So a hundred times they bowed their heads and prostrated themselves to the ground praying upon their faces. When the prayer was done, Jesus called Peter and told him and all the disciples what he had seen. And to Peter he said: "Your soul, which is greater than all the earth, through one eye sees the sun, which is a thousand times greater than all the earth." "It is true," said Peter. Then Jesus said: "So even now, through paradise you shall see God our Creator." And having said this, Jesus gave thanks to God our Lord, praying for the house of Israel and for the holy city. And everyone answered: "So be it, Lord."

## Chapter 180


One day, Jesus being in Solomon's porch, a scribe drew near to him, one of those who preached to the people, and said to him: "Master, I have many times preached to this people, and there is in my mind a passage of scripture that I cannot understand." Jesus answered: "And what is it?" The scribe said: "What God said to Abraham our father: 'I will be your great reward.' Now how can man merit?" Then Jesus rejoiced in spirit and said: "Truly you are not far from the kingdom of God. But listen to me, for I will tell you the meaning of such teaching. God being infinite, and man finite, man cannot merit God — and is this your doubt, brother?" The scribe answered, weeping: "Lord, you know my heart; speak then, for my soul desires your voice." Then Jesus said: "As God lives, man cannot merit a little breath which he receives every moment." The scribe was beside himself hearing this, and the disciples likewise marvelled, for they remembered what Jesus said, that what they gave for love of God they would receive a hundredfold. Then he said: "If one should lend you a hundred gold pieces, and you should spend those pieces, could you say to that man: 'I give you a rotten vine-leaf; give me therefore your house, for I merit it'?" The scribe answered: "No, Lord, for he ought first to pay the debt, and then, if he wants anything, he should give him good things; but what use is a rotten leaf!"

## Chapter 181


Jesus answered: "Well have you said, brother; tell me then: who created man out of nothing? Certainly it was God, to whom God gave the whole world for his benefit. But man by sinning has spent it all, for by sin all the world is contrary to man, and miserable man has nothing to give to God but works rotten with sin. For sinning every day he rots his own works — wherefore Isaiah the prophet says: 'Our righteousnesses are as a menstruous cloth.' Now how can man merit, when he cannot satisfy? And is it perhaps that man does not sin? Certainly it is that our God says by his prophet David: 'Seven times a day the just falls'; how then does the unjust fall? And if our righteousnesses are rotten, how abominable are our unrighteousnesses! As God lives, there is nothing that man ought to shun more than this saying: 'I merit.' Let man know, brother, the works of his hands, and soon he will see his merit. Every good thing that comes out of man — in truth man does it not, but God works it in man; for his being is of God who created him. What man does is to contradict God his creator and to commit sin, for which he merits not reward but torment."

## Chapter 182


"Not only has God created man, as I say, but he created him perfect. He gave him the whole world; after the departure from paradise he gave him two angels to guard him; he sent him the prophets; he granted him the law; he granted him the faith; every moment he delivers him from Satan; he willed to give him paradise; and what is more, God willed to give himself to man. Now consider whether the debt is great! To cancel it you would need to have a man created by you out of nothing, to have created as many prophets as God has sent you, with a world and a paradise, and moreover with a God great and good as our God is, and to give it all to God — so would the debt be cancelled, and only the obligation to give thanks to God would remain to you. But since you cannot create a fly, and since there is but one God who is master of all, how can you cancel your debt? Surely, if a man should lend you a hundred gold pieces, you are obliged to restore a hundred gold pieces. The meaning, then, of this, brother, is that God, since he is master of paradise and of everything, can say what pleases him and give what pleases him. Wherefore, when he said to Abraham: 'I will be your great reward,' Abraham could not say: 'God is my reward,' but must say: 'God is my gift and my debt.' So when you, brother, preach to the people, you must explain this passage thus: that God will give man such and such a thing if man works well.

"When God speaks to you, O man, and says: 'My servant, you have worked well for love of me; what reward do you seek from me, your God?' — answer: 'Lord, since I am the work of your hands, it is not fitting that there should be in me sin, which Satan loves. Therefore, Lord, for your own glory, have mercy on the works of your hands.' And if God should say: 'I have forgiven you, and now I wish to reward you,' answer: 'Lord, for what I have done I merit to be punished, and for what you have done you merit to be glorified. Therefore, Lord, punish in me what I have done, and save what you have wrought.' And if God should say: 'What punishment seems fitting to you for your sin?' answer: 'As much as all the reprobate shall suffer, O Lord.' And if God should say: 'For what reason do you seek so great a punishment, my faithful servant?' answer: 'Because each one of them, if they had received from you as much as I have received, would have served you more faithfully than I.' And if God should say: 'When will you receive this punishment, and for how long?' answer: 'Now, and without end.' As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands, such a man would be more pleasing to God than all his holy angels. For God loves true humility and hates pride." Then the scribe thanked Jesus and said to him: "Lord, let us go to the house of your servant, for your servant will give you and your disciples to eat." Jesus answered: "I will come when you promise to call me brother and not Lord, and to say that you are my brother and not my servant." The man promised, and Jesus went to his house.

## Chapter 183


While they were eating, the scribe said: "O master, you said that God loves true humility — so tell us what humility is, and how it can be true and false."

"In truth I tell you that whoever does not become as a little child shall not enter into the kingdom of heaven."

Everyone was bewildered at hearing this, and they said to one another: "Now how shall a man of thirty or forty years become a little child? Surely this is a hard saying."

Jesus answered: "As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands, my words are true. I have told you that you must become as a little child — for this is true humility. Because if you ask a little child who made the clothes he is wearing, he will answer: My father. If you ask him whose is the house where he lives, he will say: My father's. If you say: Who gives you to eat? he will answer: My father. If you say: Who taught you to walk and to speak? he will answer: My father. But if you say: Who broke your forehead, that your forehead is bound up so? he will answer: I fell down, and so broke my head. If you say: Now why did you fall? he will answer: Do you not see that I am little, so that I have not the strength to walk and run like a grown man? So my father must take me by the hand if I would walk firmly. But so that I might learn to walk well, my father left me for a little while, and I, wanting to run, fell down. If you say: Now what did your father say? he will answer: Why did you not walk quite slowly? See that in the future you do not leave my side."

## Chapter 184


"Tell me, is this true?" said Jesus.

The disciples and the scribe answered: "It is most true."

Then Jesus said: "Whoever in truth of heart acknowledges God as the author of every good, and himself as the author of sin, shall be truly humble. But whoever shall speak with the tongue as the child speaks, and shall contradict it in deeds, surely he has false humility and true pride. For pride is then at its height when it makes use of humble things, so as not to be rebuked and trampled upon by men. True humility is a lowliness of soul by which man knows himself in truth; but false humility is a mist from hell which so darkens the understanding of the soul that what a man ought to ascribe to himself he ascribes to God, and what he ought to ascribe to God he ascribes to himself. So the man of false humility will say that he is a grievous sinner, but when someone tells him that he is a sinner he will grow angry against him and will persecute him. The man of false humility will say that God has given him what he has, but that he on his part has not slept, but has done good works.

"And these Pharisees of this present time — brothers, tell me how they walk."

The scribe answered, weeping: "O master, the Pharisees of the present time have the garments and the name of Pharisees, but in their heart and their works they are Canaanites. And would to God they did not usurp such a name, for then they would not deceive the simple. O ancient time, how cruelly you have dealt with us, taking away from us the true Pharisees and leaving us the false!"

## Chapter 185


Jesus answered: "Brother, it is not time that has done this, but rather the wicked world. For in every time it is possible to serve God in truth, but by drawing near to the world — that is, to the evil customs — in every time men become bad. Now do you not know that Gehazi, servant of Elisha the prophet, with a lie, to the shame of his master, stole the money and the garments of Naaman the Syrian? And yet Elisha had a great number of Pharisees to whom God made him prophesy.

"I tell you in truth that men are so inclined to evil working, and the world so drives them to it, and Satan so entices them to evil, that the Pharisees of the present day flee from every good work and every holy example; and the example of Gehazi is enough for them to be reprobated by God."

The scribe answered: "It is most true." Whereupon Jesus said: "I would that you would narrate to me the example of Haggai and Hosea, both prophets of God, so that we may see the true Pharisee."

The scribe answered: "O master, what shall I say? Surely many do not believe it, although it is written by Daniel the prophet; but to obey you I will narrate the truth. Haggai was fifteen years old when, having sold his patrimony and given it to the poor, he went out from Anathoth to serve Obadiah the prophet. Now the aged Obadiah, who knew the humility of Haggai, used him as a book with which to teach his disciples. So he often presented him with garments and delicate foods, but Haggai always sent back the messenger, saying: 'Go and return home, for you have made a mistake. Would Obadiah send me such things now? Surely not — for he knows that I am good for nothing and only commit sins.' And Obadiah, when he had anything bad, used to give it to the one nearest to Haggai, so that he might see it. Whereupon Haggai, seeing it, would say to himself: 'Now behold, Obadiah has certainly forgotten you — for that thing is suited to me alone, because I am worse than all. And there is nothing so vile but that, receiving it from Obadiah, by whose hands God grants it to me, it is a treasure.'"

## Chapter 186


"When Obadiah wished to teach anyone to pray, he would call Haggai and say: 'Recite here your prayer, so that everyone may hear your words.' Then Haggai would say: 'Lord God of Israel, with mercy look upon your servant, who calls upon you because you have created him. Righteous Lord God, remember your righteousness and punish the sins of your servant, so that they do not pollute your work. Lord my God, I cannot ask you for the delights that you grant to your faithful servants, because I do nothing but sins. So, Lord, when you would give an infirmity to one of your servants, remember me your servant, for your glory.'"

And when Haggai did so, said the scribe, God so loved him that to everyone who in his time stood by him God gave prophecy. And nothing did Haggai ask in prayer that God withheld.

## Chapter 187


The good scribe wept as he said this, as the sailor weeps when he sees his ship broken up. And he said: "Hosea, when he went to serve God, was prince over the tribe of Naphtali, aged fourteen years. And so, having sold his patrimony and given it to the poor, he went to be disciple of Haggai. Hosea was so inflamed with charity that concerning all that was asked of him he would say: 'This God has given me for you, O brother — accept it, therefore!' For which reason he was soon left with only two garments, namely a tunic of sackcloth and a mantle of skins. He sold, I say, his patrimony and gave it to the poor, because otherwise no one would be suffered to be called a Pharisee.

"Hosea had the book of Moses, which he read with greatest earnestness. Now one day Haggai said to him: 'Hosea, who has taken away from you all that you had?' He answered: 'The book of Moses.' It happened that a disciple of a neighbouring prophet was fain to go to Jerusalem, but had not a mantle. Wherefore, having heard of the charity of Hosea, he went to find him and said to him: 'Brother, I would fain go to Jerusalem to perform a sacrifice to our God, but I have not a mantle, wherefore I know not what to do.' When he heard this, Hosea said: 'Pardon me, brother, for I have committed a great sin against you — because God has given me a mantle in order that I might give it to you, and I had forgotten. Now therefore accept it, and pray to God for me.' The man, believing this, accepted Hosea's mantle and departed.

"And when Hosea went to Haggai, Haggai said: 'Who has taken away your mantle?' Hosea replied: 'The book of Moses.' Haggai was much pleased at hearing this, because he perceived the goodness of Hosea. It happened that a poor man was stripped by robbers and left naked. Whereupon Hosea, seeing him, stripped off his own tunic and gave it to him that was naked; himself being left with a little piece of goat-skin over the private parts. Wherefore, as he came not to see Haggai, the good Haggai thought that Hosea was sick. So he went with two disciples to find him, and they found him wrapped in palm-leaves. Then said Haggai: 'Tell me now, wherefore have you not been to visit me?' Hosea answered: 'The book of Moses has taken away my tunic, and I feared to come thither without a tunic.' Whereupon Haggai gave him another tunic.

"It happened that a young man, seeing Hosea read the book of Moses, wept, and said: 'I also would learn to read if I had a book.' Hearing which, Hosea gave him the book, saying: 'Brother, this book is yours, because God has given it to me in order that I might give it to him who weeping desires a book.' The man believed it and received the book."

## Chapter 188


There was a disciple of Haggai living near Hosea. And wanting to see whether his own book was well written, he went to Hosea and said to him, "Brother, take your book, and let us see whether it is like mine." Hosea answered, "It has been taken from me." "Who took it from you?" said the disciple. Hosea answered, "The book of Moses." Hearing this, the disciple went to Haggai and said to him, "Hosea has gone mad, for he says that the book of Moses has taken the book of Moses from him." Haggai answered, "Would to God, O brother, that I were mad in this way, and that all madmen were like Hosea."

Now the robbers of Syria, having raided the land of Judea, seized the son of a poor widow, who lived near Mount Carmel, where the prophets and Pharisees dwelt. And it so happened that Hosea, having gone to cut wood, met the woman weeping, and at once he began to weep; for when he saw anyone laughing he laughed, and when he saw anyone weeping he wept. Hosea asked the woman the reason for her weeping, and she told him everything. Then Hosea said, "Come, sister, for God wills to give you your son." And the two of them went to Hebron, where Hosea sold himself and gave the money to the widow, who, not knowing how he had gotten that money, received it and bought back her son. He who had bought Hosea took him to Jerusalem, where he kept his house, not knowing Hosea.

Haggai, seeing that Hosea was not to be found, grieved over it. And the angel of God told him how he had been taken as a slave to Jerusalem. The good Haggai, hearing this, wept for the absence of Hosea as a mother weeps for the absence of her son. And he called two disciples and went to Jerusalem. And on entering the city he met Hosea by the will of God, who was laden with bread to carry it to the laborers in his master's vineyard. Recognizing him, Haggai said, "Son, why have you abandoned your old father, who seeks you in grief?" Hosea answered, "Father, I have been sold." Then Haggai said in anger, "Who was that wretch who sold you?" Hosea answered, "God forgive you, my father, for he who sold me is so good that if he were not in the world no one would become holy." "Who then is he?" said Haggai. Hosea answered, "O my father, it was the book of Moses." Then the good Haggai stood as if beside himself, and said, "Would to God, my son, that the book of Moses would sell me too, with all my children, even as it has sold you!" And Haggai went with Hosea to the house of his master, who when he saw Haggai said, "Blessed be our God, who has sent his prophet to my house," and ran to kiss his hands. Then Haggai said, "Brother, kiss the hands of your servant whom you have bought, for he is better than I." And he told him all that had happened; whereupon the master gave Hosea his freedom.

"And this is all that you sought, O master."

## Chapter 189


Then Jesus said, "This is true, for I am assured of it by God. So that everyone may know that this is the truth, in the name of God let the sun stand still, and not move for the space of twelve hours!" And this was done, to the terror of all Jerusalem and Judea.

And Jesus said to the scribe, "O brother, what do you seek to learn from me, having such knowledge? As God lives, this is enough for the salvation of man — for the humility of Haggai with the charity of Hosea fulfilled the whole law with all the prophets. Tell me, brother: when you came to question me in the temple, did you think, perhaps, that God had sent me to destroy the law and the prophets? Certain it is that God will not do this, for he is unchangeable; and therefore what God ordained as the way of man's salvation, this he caused all the prophets to say.

"As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — if the book of Moses with the book of David our father had not been corrupted by the human traditions of false Pharisees and doctors, God would not have given his word to me. And why speak I of the book of Moses and the book of David? Every prophecy have they corrupted, so that today a thing is not sought because God has commanded it; but men look to see whether the doctors say it and the Pharisees observe it — as though God could be in error and men could not err. Woe then to this faithless generation, for upon them shall come the blood of every prophet and righteous man, with the blood of Zechariah son of Barachiah, whom they slew between the temple and the altar. What prophet have they not persecuted? What righteous man have they let die a natural death? Scarcely one! And therefore now they seek to kill me. They glory in being children of Abraham, and in having the beautiful temple. As God lives — they are children of Satan, and therefore they do his will; and therefore the temple with the holy city shall go to ruin, so that of the temple there shall not remain stone upon stone."

## Chapter 190


"Tell me, brother — you who are a doctor learned in the law: the promise of the Messiah — in whom was it made to our father Abraham? In Isaac, or in Ishmael?" The scribe answered, "O master, I fear to tell you this, because the penalty is death." Then Jesus said, "Brother, I grieve that I came to eat bread in your house, since you love this present life more than God your creator; and for this reason you fear to lose your life, but fear not to lose the faith and the life eternal — which is lost when the tongue speaks contrary to what the heart knows of the law of God."

Then the good scribe, weeping, said, "O master, if I had known how to bear fruit, I would have preached many things which, so as not to raise sedition among the people, I have kept silent." Jesus answered, "Neither the people, nor the whole world, nor all the saints, nor all the angels are to be respected when God's honor is at stake. Let the whole world perish rather than offend God your creator — and do not seek to preserve it by sin, for sin destroys and does not preserve. And God is mighty to create as many worlds as the sea has sand, and more."

## Chapter 191


Then the scribe said, "Pardon me, O master, for I have sinned." Jesus said, "May God pardon you, against whom you have sinned." Then the scribe said, "I have seen an old book written by the hand of Moses and Joshua — he who made the sun stand still, as you have done — servants and prophets of God, which book is the true book of Moses. In it is written that Ishmael is the father of the Messiah, and Isaac the father of the messenger of the Messiah. And thus the book says that Moses said: 'Lord God of Israel, mighty and merciful, manifest to your servant the splendour of your glory.' And God showed him his messenger in the arms of Ishmael, and Ishmael in the arms of Abraham. And near Ishmael stood Isaac, in whose arms was a child, who with his finger pointed to the messenger of God, saying, 'This is he for whom God has created all things.' And Moses cried out with joy: 'O Ishmael, you have in your arms the whole world with paradise! Remember me, servant of God, that I may find grace in God's sight by means of your son, through whom God has made all things.'"

## Chapter 192


"In that book it is not found that God eats the flesh of cattle or sheep. In that book it is not found that God has locked up his mercy in Israel alone; but rather that God has mercy on every man who seeks God his creator in truth. All of this book I could not read, because the high priest, in whose library I was, forbade it to me, saying that an Ishmaelite had written it."

Then Jesus said, "See that you never again keep back the truth, for in the faith of the Messiah God shall give salvation to men, and without it none shall be saved."

And there Jesus ended his discourse. And as they were eating, behold Mary — who wept at the feet of Jesus — entered the house of Nicodemus, for that was the name of the scribe; and weeping she placed herself at the feet of Jesus, saying, "Lord, your servant, who through you has found mercy with God, has a sister and a brother who now lies sick in danger of death."

Jesus answered, "Where is your house? Tell me, for I will come to pray to God for his health." Mary answered, "Bethany is my brother's and my sister's, for my own house is Magdala; so my brother is in Bethany." Jesus said to the woman, "Go quickly to your brother's house, and wait for me there, for I will come to heal him. And fear not, for he shall not die."

The woman departed, and having gone to Bethany found that her brother had died that day; so they laid him in the sepulchre of their fathers.

## Chapter 193


Jesus stayed two days in the house of Nicodemus, and the third day he left for Bethany. And when he was near the city he sent two disciples ahead to announce his coming to Mary. She ran out of the city and found Jesus, and weeping said: "Lord, you said that my brother would not die; and now he has been buried four days. Would to God you had come before I called you, for then he would not have died."

Jesus answered: "Your brother is not dead, but sleeps; therefore I come to wake him."

Mary answered, weeping: "Lord, from such a sleep he shall be awakened on the day of judgment by the angel of God sounding the trumpet."

Jesus said: "Mary, believe me, he shall rise before that day, for God has given me power over his sleep; and I tell you in truth that he is not dead — for only he is dead who dies without finding mercy with God."

Mary returned quickly to announce the coming of Jesus to Martha her sister. There had gathered at the death of Lazarus a great crowd of Jews from Jerusalem, and many scribes and Pharisees. Martha, having heard from Mary her sister of the coming of Jesus, arose in haste and ran outside, and the multitude of Jews, scribes, and Pharisees followed her to comfort her, for they thought she was going to the sepulchre to weep for her brother. And when she arrived where Jesus had spoken with Mary, Martha weeping said: "Lord, would to God you had been here, for then my brother would not have died!"

Mary then came up weeping, whereupon Jesus shed tears, and sighing said: "Where have you laid him?" They answered: "Come and see."

The Pharisees said among themselves: "Now this man, who raised the son of the widow at Nain — why did he let this man die, having said that he would not die?"

Jesus, having come to the sepulchre where everyone was weeping, said: "Do not weep, for Lazarus sleeps, and I have come to wake him."

The Pharisees said among themselves: "Would to God that you slept so!"

Then Jesus said: "My hour is not yet come; but when it shall come, I shall sleep in like manner and shall be speedily awakened."

Again Jesus said: "Take away the stone of the sepulchre."

Martha said: "Lord, he stinks, for he has been dead four days."

Jesus said: "Why then have I come here, Martha? Do you not believe me, that I shall wake him?"

Martha answered: "I know that you are the holy one of God, who has sent you into this world."

Then Jesus, having lifted his hands to heaven, said: "Lord God of Abraham, God of Ishmael and of Isaac, God of our fathers — have mercy upon the affliction of these women, and give glory to your holy name."

And when everyone had answered "Amen," Jesus said with a loud voice: "Lazarus, come forth!"

Whereupon the dead man arose, and Jesus said to his disciples: "Loose him" — for he was bound in the grave-clothes with the napkin over his face, even as our fathers are accustomed to bury.

A great multitude of Jews and some Pharisees believed in Jesus, for the miracle was great. Those who remained in their unbelief departed and went to Jerusalem and reported to the chief of the priests the resurrection of Lazarus, and how many had become Nazarenes — for so they called those who through the word of God which Jesus preached did penance.

## Chapter 194


The scribes and Pharisees took counsel with the high priest to slay Lazarus, for many renounced their traditions and believed in the word of Jesus, because the miracle of Lazarus was great — seeing that Lazarus conversed with men, and ate and drank. But because he was powerful, having a following in Jerusalem, and possessing with his sisters Magdala and Bethany, they knew not what to do.

Jesus entered into Bethany, into the house of Lazarus, and Martha with Mary ministered to him. Mary, sitting one day at the feet of Jesus, was listening to his words — whereupon Martha said to Jesus: "Lord, do you not see that my sister takes no care of you, for she does not provide what you and your disciples must eat?"

Jesus answered: "Martha, Martha, see to what you ought to do, for Mary has chosen a part which shall not be taken from her for ever."
