Sitting at table, Jesus with a great multitude that believed in him spoke, saying: "Brothers, I have little time to stay with you, for the time is near when I shall depart from the world. Wherefore remember the words of God spoken to Ezekiel the prophet, saying: 'As I, your God, live for ever — the soul that sins shall die. But if the sinner does penance, he shall not die, but live.'

"Wherefore the present death is not death, but rather the end of a long death — for even as the body, separated from sense, although it has the soul in it, when it is in a swoon has no other advantage over the dead that is buried, except that the buried awaits God to raise him, while he that is in a swoon awaits sense to return. Look, then, upon the present life, which is dead for not having sense of God."

## Chapter 195


"Those who shall believe in me shall not die for ever, for through my word they shall perceive God within them, and therefore shall work out their salvation. What is death but an act which nature does by commandment of God? As it would be if one held a bird tied, and held the cord in his hand; when the master wants the bird to fly away, what does he do? Assuredly he commands the hand naturally to open, and so straightway the bird flies away. Our soul, as David the prophet says, is as a sparrow freed from the snare of the fowler, when man stands under the protection of God. And our life is like a cord with which nature holds the soul bound to the body and the human sense. When God wills and commands nature to open, life is broken and the soul escapes into the hands of the angel whom God has appointed to receive souls.

"Friends, do not weep when a friend dies, for our God has so willed it; but weep without end when he sins, for the soul dies, seeing that it separates itself from God, the true life. For if the body without communion of the soul is horrible, much more frightful is the soul without union of God — who with his grace and mercy makes it beautiful and gives it life."

And having said this, Jesus gave thanks to God, whereupon Lazarus said: "Lord, this house belongs to God my creator, with all that he has given me in keeping for the service of the poor; wherefore, since you are poor and have a great number of disciples — when it pleases you, and as much as it pleases you, come to dwell here, for the servant of God will minister to you as much as shall be needful, for love of God."

## Chapter 196


Jesus rejoiced when he heard this, and said: "See now how good a thing it is to die! Lazarus has died only once, and has learned such teaching as the wisest men in the world, who have grown old among books, do not know. Would to God that every man might die only once and return to the world, like Lazarus, so that men might learn to live!"

John answered: "O master, is it permitted to me to speak a word?"

"Speak a thousand," answered Jesus, "for just as man is bound to dispense his goods in the service of God, so is he bound to dispense teaching; and the obligation is so much the greater, inasmuch as the word can raise a soul to penitence, while goods cannot bring back life to the dead. Wherefore he is a murderer who has the means to help a poor man, and, not helping him, the poor man dies of hunger; but greater is the murder of him who by the word of God could convert the sinner to penitence, and converts him not, but stands — as God says — like a dumb dog; against whom God says: 'The soul of the sinner that shall perish because you have hidden my word from him — I will require it at your hands, O unfaithful servant.' In what state, then, are now the scribes and Pharisees, who have the key and will not enter, but rather hinder those who would enter into eternal life!

"You ask me, O John, leave to speak a word, having listened to a hundred thousand of mine. In truth I tell you, I am bound to listen to you ten times for one that you have listened to me. And he who will not listen to others, every time he speaks he shall sin — seeing that we ought to do to others what we would for ourselves, and not do to others what we would not wish to receive."

Then said John: "O master, why has not God granted this to men — that they die once and return like Lazarus, so that they might learn to know themselves and their creator?"

## Chapter 197


Jesus answered: "Tell me, John: there was a head of a household who gave a perfect axe to one of his servants, to cut down the wood that blocked the view of his house. But the labourer forgot the axe, and said: 'If the master would give me an old axe, I would easily cut down the wood.' Tell me, O John, what said the master? Surely, being angry, he took the old axe and struck him on the head, saying: 'You lazy scoundrel! I gave you an axe with which you could cut down the wood without toil, and you ask for this one, which is used with great toil, and whatever it cuts is wasted, good for nothing. I want you to cut the wood in such a way that your work is good.' Is this true?"

John answered: "It is most true."

"'As I live for ever,' says God, 'I have given a good axe to every man, which is the sight of the burial of a dead man. Those who wield it well remove without pain the wood of sins from their heart. Wherefore they receive my grace and mercy, giving them the reward of eternal life for their good working. But he who forgets that he is mortal, seeing others die at every time, and says: "If I saw the other life, I would do good" — my fury shall be upon him, and I will smite him so with death that he shall never receive any good.'"

"O John," said Jesus, "how great is the advantage of him who from the fall of others learns to stand upright!"

## Chapter 198


Then Lazarus said: "Master, truly I tell you, I cannot understand what punishment he deserves who, every time he sees the dead carried to the tomb, does not fear God our creator — and who, for the things of this world, which he must leave behind entirely, offends his creator, who gave him everything." Then Jesus said to his disciples: "You call me master, and you do well, for God teaches you through my mouth. But what will you call Lazarus? Truly, here is the master of all the masters who teach the doctrine of this world. I have taught you how you must live well, but Lazarus will teach you how to die well. As God lives, he has received the gift of prophecy; so listen to his words, which are truth. And you must listen to him all the more, because good living is vain when one dies badly."

Lazarus said: "O master, I thank you for making the truth prized; so God will give you great merit." Then he who writes said: "O master, how does Lazarus speak the truth in telling you 'you will have merit,' when you said to Nicodemus that man merits nothing but punishment? So will you be punished by God?"

Jesus answered: "May it please God that I receive punishment from God in this world, for I have not served him as faithfully as I am bound to. But God has loved me so much in his mercy that he has taken every punishment away from me — so much so that I shall be tormented only in another person. For punishment was fitting for me, since men have called me God. But since I have confessed, not only that I am not God, as is the truth, but I have also confessed that I am not the Messiah — so God has taken the punishment away from me, and he will make a wicked one suffer it in my name, so that only the shame will be mine. So I tell you, my Barnabas: when a man speaks of what God will give to his neighbor, let him say that his neighbor merits it. But let him be careful, when he speaks of what God will give to himself, to say: 'God will give me' — and let him be careful not to say 'I merit,' for God is pleased to grant his mercy to his servants when they confess that they merit hell for their sins."

## Chapter 199


"God is so rich in mercy that, though the water of a thousand seas — if so many could be found — could not put out one spark of the flames of hell, one single tear of him who grieves at having offended God puts out the whole of hell, by the great mercy with which God helps him. So God, to confound Satan and to show his generosity, wanted every good work of his faithful servant to be called 'merit' before his mercy — and wanted it to be spoken this way of his neighbor. But let a man, of himself, be careful not to say 'I merit,' for he would be condemned."

## Chapter 200


Jesus turned to Lazarus and said to him: "Brother, I must stay in the world a short time; so when I am near your house I will never go anywhere else, for you will serve me not for love of me but for love of God." It was near the Passover of the Jews, so Jesus said to his disciples: "Let us go to Jerusalem to eat the Passover lamb." And he sent Peter and John to the city, saying: "You will find a she-ass near the gate of the city, with a colt. Loose her and bring her here, for I need to ride on her into Jerusalem. And if anyone asks you, saying 'Why do you loose her?' tell them: 'The master needs her,' and they will let you bring her." The disciples went and found what Jesus had told them, and so they brought the she-ass with the colt. So the disciples put their mantles on the colt, on which Jesus rode. And it happened that, when the men of Jerusalem heard that Jesus of Nazareth was coming, the men went out with their children, eager to see Jesus, carrying in their hands branches of palm and of olive, singing: "Blessed be he who comes to us in the name of God! Hosanna, son of David!" When Jesus came to the city, the men spread their garments under the feet of the ass, singing: "Blessed be he who comes to us in the name of the Lord God! Hosanna, son of David!" The Pharisees rebuked Jesus, saying: "Do you not see what these say? Make them be silent." Jesus said to them: "As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — if men should be silent, the stones would cry out against the unbelief of wicked sinners." And when Jesus had said this, all the stones of Jerusalem cried out with a great noise: "Blessed be he who comes to us in the name of the Lord God!" Nevertheless the Pharisees remained in their unbelief, and gathering together among themselves they took counsel to catch him in his speech.

## Chapter 201


When Jesus had entered the temple, the scribes and Pharisees brought to him a woman caught in adultery. They said among themselves: "If he saves her, it is against the law of Moses — so we have him as guilty. And if he condemns her, it is against his own teaching, which preaches mercy." So coming to Jesus they said: "Master, we have found this woman in adultery. Moses commanded that she be stoned; but you, what do you say?" Jesus stooped down and with his finger made a mirror in the earth, in which everyone saw his own iniquities. As they still pressed for the answer, Jesus stood up, and pointing to the mirror with his finger said: "He among you who is without sin, let him be the first to stone her." And again he stooped down, making the mirror. The men, seeing this, went out one by one, beginning with the eldest, for they were ashamed to see their abominations. Jesus stood up, and seeing no one but the woman said: "Woman, where are they who condemned you?" The woman answered, weeping: "Lord, they are gone; and if you will pardon me, as God lives, I will sin no more." Then Jesus said: "Blessed be God! Go in peace and sin no more, for God has not sent me to condemn you."

So Jesus, calling together the scribes and Pharisees, said to them: "Tell me: if one of you had a hundred sheep, and one of them were lost — would you not go seeking it, leaving the ninety-nine? And having found it, would you not put it on your shoulders? And calling your neighbors together, would you not say: 'Rejoice with me, for I have found the sheep I had lost'? Certainly you would do this. Now tell me: will our God love man less — man, for whom he made the world? As God lives — so there is joy before the angels of God over one sinner who repents, for sinners make known the mercy of God."

## Chapter 202


"Tell me: by whom is the physician more loved — by those who have never had any sickness, or by those whom the physician has healed of a grave sickness?" The Pharisees said to him: "And how will the healthy man love the physician? Certainly he will love him only for not falling sick — and, not knowing sickness, he will love the physician little." Then with force of spirit Jesus spoke, saying: "As God lives — your own tongues condemn your pride, for our God is loved more by the sinner who repents, knowing the great mercy of God upon himself, than by the righteous. For the righteous man does not know the mercy of God. So there is more joy before the angels of God over one sinner who repents than over ninety-nine righteous. Where are the righteous in our time? As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands — great is the number of the righteous-unrighteous, for their condition is equal to Satan's." The scribes and Pharisees answered: "We are sinners, so God will have mercy on us." And they said this to tempt him — for the scribes and Pharisees count it the greatest insult to be called sinners. Then Jesus said: "I fear that you are righteous-unrighteous. For if you have sinned and deny your sin, calling yourselves righteous, you are unrighteous. And if in your heart you hold yourselves righteous, and with your tongue you say you are sinners — then you are doubly righteous-unrighteous." So the scribes and Pharisees, hearing this, were confounded and went away, leaving Jesus with his disciples in peace.

And they went into the house of Simon the leper, from whom he had cleansed the leprosy. The citizens gathered the sick to Simon's house and begged Jesus for the healing of the sick. Then Jesus, knowing that his hour was near, said: "Call as many sick as there are, for God is mighty and merciful to heal them." They answered: "We know of no others who are sick here in Jerusalem." Weeping, Jesus answered: "O Jerusalem, O Israel! I weep over you, for you do not know your visitation — for I wanted to gather you to the love of God your creator, as the hen gathers her chicks under her wings, and you would not. So this is what God says to you —"

## Chapter 203


"O city of hard heart and perverse of mind! I have sent you my servant so that you would turn to your heart and do penance. But you, O city of confusion, have forgotten what I did over Egypt and over Pharaoh for love of you, O Israel. Many times you weep so that my servant may heal your body of sickness, and you seek to kill my servant because he seeks to heal your soul of sin. Shall you then alone remain unpunished by me? Shall you then live eternally? And shall your pride deliver you from my hands? Certainly not. For I will bring princes with an army against you, and they will surround you with power, and in such a way will I betray you into their hands that your pride shall fall into hell. I will not pardon the old men nor the widows. I will not pardon the children, but I will give you all to hunger, sword, and derision. And the temple, upon which I looked with mercy, I will desolate together with the city, so that you will be a fable and a derision and a proverb among the nations. Thus is my wrath fixed upon you, and my indignation does not sleep."

## Chapter 204


Having said this, Jesus said again: "You do not know that there are other sick folk. As God lives, they are fewer in Jerusalem, those who have a sound soul, than those who are sick in body. And so that you may know the truth I say to you, O sick folk, in the name of God let the sickness depart from you." And having said this, immediately they were healed. The men wept, having heard the wrath of God upon Jerusalem, and asked for mercy. Jesus said: "If Jerusalem shall weep for her sins and do penance, walking in my ways, says God, I will not remember her iniquities any more, and I will not do to her any of the evil which I have said. But Jerusalem weeps for her ruin and not for dishonoring me, with which she has made my name blasphemed among the nations. Therefore my fury is kindled much more. As I live eternally, if Job, Abraham, Samuel, David, and Daniel my servants, with Moses, should pray for this people, my wrath upon Jerusalem will not be appeased." And having said this, Jesus withdrew into the house, everyone remaining in fear.

## Chapter 205


While Jesus was supping with his disciples in the house of Simon the leper, behold Mary the sister of Lazarus entered into the house, and having broken a vessel she poured ointment over the head and garment of Jesus. Seeing this, Judas the traitor wanted to hinder Mary from doing such a work, saying: "Go and sell the ointment and bring the money, that I may give it to the poor." Jesus said: "Why do you hinder her? Let her be, for the poor you shall have always with you, but me you shall not have always." Judas answered: "O master, this ointment could be sold for three hundred pieces of money; now see how many poor would be helped." Jesus answered: "O Judas, I know your heart; have patience therefore, and I will give you everything." Everyone ate with fear, and the disciples grieved, for they knew that Jesus must soon depart from them. But Judas was indignant, because he knew he was losing thirty pieces of money for the ointment not sold, seeing that he stole the tenth of all that was given to Jesus.

He went to find the high priest, who assembled in council priests, scribes, and Pharisees. To them Judas spoke, saying: "What will you give me, and I will betray into your hands Jesus, who wanted to make himself king of Israel?" They answered: "Now how will you give him into our hand?" Judas said: "When I shall know that he goes outside the city to pray, I will tell you, and I will lead you to where he shall be found; for taking him in the city will not be without sedition." The high priest answered: "If you give him into our hand we will give you thirty pieces of gold, and I will do you such good as you shall see."

## Chapter 206


When day was come, Jesus went up to the temple with a great multitude of people. Whereupon the high priest drew near to him, saying: "Tell me, O Jesus, have you forgotten what you confessed, that you are not God, nor son of God, nor even the Messiah?" Jesus answered: "Certainly not, for I have not forgotten; for this is my confession, which I shall bear before the tribunal of God on the day of judgment. For all that is written in the book of Moses is most true, because God our Creator is one alone, and I am the servant of God and desire to serve the messenger of God whom you call the messiah."¹ The high priest said: "Then what is the use of coming to the temple with so great a multitude of people? Do you seek perhaps to make yourself king of Israel? Beware lest some danger befall you." Jesus answered: "If I sought my own glory and desired my portion in this world, when the people of Nain wanted to make me their king I would not have fled. Believe me in truth that I seek nothing in this world." The high priest said: "Then we lack one thing to know concerning the messiah." And then the priests, scribes, and Pharisees made a circle around Jesus. Jesus answered: "What is that thing which you seek to know about the messiah? Perhaps it is the lie? Of a truth I will not tell you the lie; for if I had told the lie I would have been adored by you and by the scribes and Pharisees with all Israel. But because I tell you the truth you hate me and seek to kill me." The high priest said: "Now we know that you have the devil upon your back, for you are a Samaritan and have no respect for the priest of God."

¹ **SEAM NOTE.** The Italian reads *he io son seruo di DIG he dessidero di seruire al nontio di DIG e il qualle chiamate messia* — translated exactly as written above: "I am the servant of God and desire to serve the messenger of God whom you call the messiah." Robert's standing suspicion (recorded 2026-10-10) is that the unnamed-*nontio* and named-Muhammad passages in this Gospel may be later insertions rather than the Gospel's own voice — a question never decided, never smoothed, never deleted. The Quran-contradiction fork stays open: the reading is kept as printed and the suspicion is not resolved in either direction.

## Chapter 207


Jesus answered: "As God lives, I have not the devil upon my back, but I seek to cast out the devil. Therefore the devil stirs up the world against me, because I am not of this world. But I seek that God may be glorified, who has sent me into the world. Hear me therefore, for I will tell you who has the devil upon his back. As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands, he who works according to the will of the devil — he has the devil upon his back, who has put upon him the bridle of his will and rules him at his pleasure, making him run into every iniquity. Even as a garment changes its name with the change of its owner, though it is all one cloth, so men, though they are all of one matter, are different by the works of him who works in man. If I have — as I know — sinned, why do you not rebuke me as a brother, instead of hating me as an enemy? In truth the members of a body help one another when they are united with the head, and those that are divided from the head do not help it; for the hands of one body do not feel the pain of the feet of another body, but only of the body in which they are united. As God lives, in whose presence my soul stands, he who fears and loves God his Creator has the feeling of mercy over those over whom God his head has mercy. And seeing that God does not want the death of the sinner, but awaits every one to penance, if you were of that body in which I am incorporated, as God lives you would help me to work according to my head."

## Chapter 208


"If I work iniquity, reprove me, and God will love you, because you will be doing his will. But if no one can reprove me of sin, it is a sign that you are not sons of Abraham as you call yourselves, nor are you incorporated with that head with which Abraham was incorporated. As God lives, so greatly did Abraham love God that he not only broke the false idols and abandoned his father and his mother, but was willing to kill his own son to obey God."

The high priest answered: "This I ask of you, and I do not seek to kill you; but tell me, who was this son of Abraham?"

Jesus answered: "The zeal of your honor, O my God, burns me, and I cannot be silent; therefore I tell you in truth that the son of Abraham was Ishmael, from whom must descend the messiah promised to Abraham, to bless in him all the tribes of the earth."

The high priest, hearing this, grew angry and cried out: "Let us stone this impious man, for he is an Ishmaelite and has blasphemed against Moses and against the law of God." Whereupon every scribe and Pharisee, with the elders of the people, took stones to stone Jesus, who vanished from their eyes and went out of the temple. And through the great desire they had to kill Jesus, blinded by fury and savage hatred, they struck one another, so that a thousand men died there; and they polluted the holy temple.

The disciples and the believers, who saw Jesus go out of the temple — for to them he was not hidden — followed him to the house of Simon. Then Nicodemus came there and advised Jesus to go out of Jerusalem, beyond the brook Cedron, saying: "Lord, I have a garden with a house beyond the brook Cedron; therefore I pray you, go there with some of your disciples to stay there until this hatred of our priests passes. For I will minister to you what is necessary; and the multitude of the disciples, leave them here in the house of Simon and in my house, for God will provide for everything." This Jesus did, and he desired to have with him only the twelve first-called apostles.

## Chapter 209


At this time, while Mary the virgin, mother of Jesus, was standing in prayer, the angel Gabriel visited her and told her of the persecution of her son, saying: "Fear not, Mary, for God will protect him from the world." Wherefore Mary, weeping, departed from Nazareth and came to Jerusalem, to the house of Mary Salome her sister, seeking her son. But since he had secretly retired beyond the brook of Cedron, she could not see him any more in this world, except after the deed of shame. For the angel Gabriel, with the angel Michael, Raphael, and Uriel, by commission of God, brought him to her.

## Chapter 210


When the confusion in the temple ceased with the departure of Jesus, the high priest ascended on high, and having made a sign of silence with his hands, he said: "Brothers, what are we doing? Do you not see that this man has deceived the whole world with his diabolical art? Now, how did he vanish, if he is not a magician? Certainly, if he were holy and a prophet, he would not blaspheme against God and against Moses his servant and against the messiah who is the hope of Israel. And what shall I say? He has blasphemed all our priesthood. Therefore in truth I tell you that if he is not removed from the world, Israel will be polluted, and our God will give us over to the nations. Behold now how through him this holy temple is polluted." And the high priest spoke in such a way that many turned away from Jesus, so that the secret persecution was turned into an open one — so that the high priest went in person to Herod and to the Roman governor, accusing Jesus of wanting to make himself king of Israel; and of this they had false witnesses. Whereupon a general council was held against Jesus, because the Roman decree made them afraid — seeing that twice the senate had sent a decree concerning Jesus. In one decree it was forbidden, on pain of life, that no one should call Jesus of Nazareth, prophet of the Jews, either God or son of God; in the other it was forbidden, under capital sentence, that anyone should contend on account of Jesus of Nazareth, prophet of the Jews. Wherefore on this account there was a great division among them. For some wanted that they should write again to Rome against Jesus; others said that Jesus should be left alone, with no regard for his talk, as that of a fool; others alleged the great miracles that he did. But the high priest spoke, that under pain of anathema no one should say a word in defense of Jesus; and he spoke to Herod and to the governor, saying: "In any case we have a bad bargain in our hands, for if we kill this sinner we have acted against the decree of Caesar, and if we let him live and he makes himself king, how will the matter go?" Then Herod rose up and threatened the governor, saying: "Beware, lest through your favoring this man this nation rebel; for I will accuse you before Caesar as a rebel." Then the governor feared the senate and made peace with Herod, for before they hated one another to the death; and they united as one for the death of Jesus. And they said to the high priest: "Whenever you know where the evildoer is, send to us, for we will give you the soldiers." This was done to fulfill the prophecy of David, who had foretold of Jesus, prophet of Israel, saying: "The princes and kings of the earth are united against the holy one of Israel, because he announces to them the salvation of the world." Whereupon on that day a general search was made for Jesus throughout Jerusalem.

## Chapter 211


While Jesus was in the house of Nicodemus, beyond the brook Cedron, he comforted his disciples, saying: "The hour is near when I shall depart from the world. Therefore console yourselves and do not be sad, seeing that where I go I shall feel no tribulation. Now, will you be my friends if you are sad at my good? No, certainly, but rather enemies. When the world rejoices, be sad, because the rejoicing of the world is turned into weeping; but your sadness shall be turned into joy, and your joy no one shall take from you. For the rejoicing that the heart feels in God its creator, the whole world cannot take it away. See that you do not forget the words which God has spoken to you by my mouth. See to it that you are my witnesses against everyone who shall corrupt the witness that I have witnessed with my gospel against the world, and against the lovers of the world."

## Chapter 212


And lifting his hands to the Lord, he prayed, saying: "Lord our God, God of Abraham, God of Ishmael and Isaac, God of our fathers, have mercy on those whom you have given me, and save them from the world. I do not say, take them from the world, because it is necessary that they bear witness against those who shall corrupt my gospel. But I pray you, keep them from evil, so that on the day of your judgment they may come with me to bear witness against the world and against the house of Israel, which has corrupted your testament. Lord God, strong and jealous, who avenges idolatry against the sons of idolatrous fathers, even to the fourth generation, curse eternally everyone who shall corrupt my gospel which you gave me, by writing that I am your son. For I, clay and dust, am a servant of your servants, and never have I thought myself to be your good servant; for I cannot give you anything for what you have given me, because everything is yours. Lord God, merciful, who shows mercy for a thousand generations upon those who fear you, have mercy on those who believe the words of mine which you have given me. For as you are true God, so the word that I have spoken is true, for it is yours — seeing that I have always spoken as one who reads, who cannot read except what is written in the book that he reads; so have I spoken what you have told me. Lord God, savior, save those whom you have given me, so that Satan may not be able to do anything against them; and save not only them, but everyone who shall believe in them. Lord, generous and rich in mercy, grant to your servant to be in the congregation of your messenger on the day of judgment; and not only me, but everyone whom you have given me, with all those who shall believe in me through their preaching. And do this, Lord, for your own sake, so that Satan may not boast himself against you, Lord. Lord God, who with your providence provided everything necessary for your people of Israel, be mindful of all the tribes of the earth, which you have promised to bless through your messenger, for whom you created the world. Have mercy on the world, and send speedily your messenger, that Satan, your enemy, may lose his empire." And having said this, Jesus said three times: "So be it, Lord God, great and merciful." And everyone answered, weeping: "So be it" — all save Judas, for he believed nothing.

## Chapter 213


When the day came for eating the lamb, Nicodemus secretly sent the lamb to the garden for Jesus and his disciples, telling them all that Herod had decreed with the governor and the high priest. At this Jesus rejoiced in spirit, saying: "Blessed be your holy name, O Lord, because you have not cut me off from the number of your servants who have been persecuted by the world and slain. I thank you, my God, because I have finished your work." And turning to Judas he said to him: "Friend, why do you wait? My time is near — go, then, and do what you must do." The disciples thought that Jesus was sending Judas to buy something for the day of the Passover; but Jesus knew that Judas was betraying him, and, longing to depart from the world, he spoke as follows.

Judas answered: "Lord, let me eat, and I will go."

"Let us eat," said Jesus, "for I have greatly longed to eat this lamb before I am parted from you." And rising, he took a towel and tied it round his waist; and having put water into a basin, he set himself to wash his disciples' feet, beginning with Judas. When Jesus came to Peter, Peter said: "Lord, do you want to wash my feet?"

Jesus answered: "What I am doing you do not know now, but you will know hereafter."

Peter answered: "You will never wash my feet."

Then Jesus stood up and said: "Neither will you come in my company on the day of judgement."

Peter answered: "Lord, wash not my feet only, but my hands and my head."

When the disciples were washed and sat down at table to eat, Jesus said: "I have washed you, yet you are not all clean — for the water of the sea will not wash him who does not believe in me." This Jesus said because he knew who was betraying him. The disciples were saddened at these words, when Jesus said again: "I tell you in truth, one of you will betray me, so that I will be sold like a sheep. But woe to him — for he will fulfil what our father David said of such a man: that he will fall into the pit which he had prepared for another."

At this the disciples looked at one another, saying sorrowfully: "Who will be the traitor?"

Then Judas said: "Will it be I, O Master?"

Jesus answered: "You have said it yourself — who will be the one to betray me." And the eleven apostles did not take his meaning.

When the lamb was eaten, the devil came upon Judas, and he went out of the house; to him Jesus said again: "Do quickly what you must do."

## Chapter 214


When he had gone out of the house, Jesus withdrew into the garden to pray, as was his custom: to pray a hundred times, bending his knees and casting himself down upon his face.

Judas, then, knowing the place where Jesus was with his disciples, went to the high priest and said: "If you will give me what was promised, this night I will give into your hand the Jesus you are looking for, who is alone with eleven companions."

The high priest answered: "How much are you asking?"

Judas said: "Thirty gold coins."

Then straightway the high priest counted out the money to him, and sent a Pharisee to the governor to fetch soldiers, and to Herod; and they gave a legion of them, because they feared the people. So they took their weapons, and with lights and lanterns on poles went out of Jerusalem.

## Chapter 215


When the soldiers with Judas drew near to the place where Jesus was, Jesus heard the coming of many people; so in fear he withdrew into the house, and the eleven were sleeping. Then God, seeing the danger of His servant, commanded Gabriel, Michael, Rafael, and Uriel, His ministers, to take Jesus out of the world. The holy angels came and took Jesus out through the window that looks toward the south, and carried him away; and they set him in the third heaven, in the company of angels blessing God for ever.

## Chapter 216


Judas rushed in ahead of them all into the room from which Jesus had been taken up, and the eleven were sleeping. Then the wonderful God worked wonderfully, so that Judas was so changed in his speech and in his face, made like to Jesus, that we believed him to be Jesus. And he, having wakened us, was asking where the Master was. So we, astonished, answered: "You, Lord, are our master — have you now forgotten us?"

And he, smiling, said: "Now you are fools, that you do not know me to be Judas Iscariot!"

And as he said this the soldiers came in and laid hands on Judas, because he was in all things like to Jesus.

We, having heard Judas's words and seen the multitude of soldiers, fled as men beside themselves. And John, who was wrapped in a linen sheet, woke from sleep and fled; and a soldier seized him by the linen sheet, so he left the sheet and escaped naked — for God heard the prayer of Jesus, and saved the eleven from evil.

## Chapter 217


The soldiers seized Judas and bound him, not without mockery — for he truthfully denied that he was Jesus, and the soldiers, mocking him, said: "Sir, do not be afraid — we have come to make you king of Israel, and we have bound you because we know you refuse the kingdom."

Judas answered: "Now you have lost your wits! You have come to take Jesus of Nazareth with weapons and lanterns as if he were a robber — and me, who guided you here, you have bound, to make me king!" Then the soldiers lost patience, and with fists and kicks they began to pay Judas back in kind, and led him with fury to Jerusalem.

John and Peter followed the soldiers from afar, and they told him who writes that they saw every questioning put to Judas by the high priest and by the council of the Pharisees, who were gathered to put Jesus to death. At this Judas spoke many mad words, so that everyone was filled with laughter, believing that he was truly Jesus and that for fear of death he was feigning madness. Then the scribes bound his eyes with a bandage and, mocking him, said: "Jesus, prophet of the Nazarenes — for so they called those who believed in Jesus — tell us, who was it that struck you?" And they slapped him and spat in his face.

When morning came, the great council of scribes and elders of the people gathered, and the high priest with the Pharisees looked for false witnesses against Judas, believing him to be Jesus — and they did not find what they were looking for. And why do I even say that the chief priests believed Judas to be Jesus? All the disciples, together with him who writes, believed the same. And what is more, the poor virgin mother of Jesus, with his relatives and friends, believed it, so that everyone's sorrow was beyond telling. As God lives — he who writes forgot all that Jesus had told him: that he would be taken up from the world, and that he would suffer in a third person, and that he would not die until near the end of the world. So he went with the mother of Jesus and with John to the cross.

The high priest had the bound Judas brought before him, and questioned him about his disciples and his teaching. At this Judas, as if out of his senses, answered nothing to the point. So the high priest put him under oath by the living God of Israel to tell the truth.

Judas answered: "I have told you that I am Judas Iscariot, who promised to give Jesus of Nazareth into your hands — and you, I do not know by what trick, are beside yourselves, for you will have it at all costs that I am Jesus."

The high priest answered: "O wicked deceiver — you have deceived all Israel, from Galilee all the way here to Jerusalem, with your teaching and false miracles; and now do you think to escape the punishment you deserve by pretending to be mad? As God lives, you will not escape it!" And having said this, he ordered his servants to beat him with slaps and kicks, to knock his wits back into his head. The mockery the high priest's servants then made of him passes belief — for they strained after new cruelties to please the council. So they dressed him up as a juggler, and with hands and feet mauled him so that it would have moved the Canaanites to pity if they had seen him so. But the chief priests and Pharisees and elders of the people had their hearts so hardened against Jesus that, believing Judas to be truly Jesus, they took pleasure in seeing him treated so.

Afterwards they led him bound to the governor, who in secret loved Jesus. He, believing Judas to be Jesus, had him brought into his private room and spoke with him, asking him why the chief priests with the people had given him up into his hands.

Judas answered: "If I tell you the truth, you will not believe me — for perhaps you are deceived, as the chief priests and the Pharisees are deceived."

The governor answered — thinking he wanted to speak about the Law: "Do you not know that I am no Jew? But the chief priests with the elders of your people have given you into my hand. Tell us the truth, then, so that I may do what is just — for I have power to set you free and to put you to death."

Judas answered: "Sir, believe me — if you put me to death you will commit a great sin, for you will kill an innocent man: for I am Judas Iscariot and not Jesus, who is a magician and by his art has changed me into this."

When the governor heard this he was greatly amazed, and tried to set him free. So the governor came out, and smiling said: "One thing is certain — one of two — and in either case this man is not worthy of death, but of pity. This man says," said the governor, "that he is not Jesus but a certain Judas who led the soldiers to take Jesus, and that Jesus the Galilean has by his magic art changed him into this. If this is true, it would be a great sin to kill him, since he would be innocent. But if he is Jesus and denies it, then surely he has lost his mind — and it would be a wicked thing to kill a madman."

Then the chief priests and elders of the people, with the scribes and Pharisees, shouted loudly, saying: "He is Jesus of Nazareth — we know him! For if he were not the evildoer, we would not have given him into your hands. Nor is he mad — he is full of malice: with this trick he is trying to slip out of our hands, and the uprising he would stir up by escaping would be worse than the first."

Pilate — for that was the governor's name — to be rid of such a case, said: "This man is a Galilean, and Herod is king of Galilee; it is not for me, then, to judge such a case — take him to Herod." So they led Judas to Herod, who had long wanted Jesus to come to his house. But Jesus had never been willing to go to his house, because Herod was a Gentile who worshipped the false and lying gods, living like the unclean Gentiles.

When Judas had been brought there, Herod questioned him about many things, to which Judas gave senseless answers, denying that he was Jesus. Then Herod mocked him with all his court, and had him dressed in white as fools are dressed, and sent him back to Pilate, saying to him: "Do not fail in justice to the people of Israel!" And Herod wrote this because the chief priests and scribes and the Pharisees had given him a good sum of money. The governor, having learned this from a servant of Herod, wanting to gain money too, pretended he wished to set Judas free. So he had him whipped by his servants, who had been paid by the scribes to kill him under the whips. But God, who had decreed what would happen, kept Judas for the cross, so that he would suffer the horrible death into which he had sold another man. God did not let Judas die under the whips, even though the soldiers whipped him so cruelly that his body ran with blood. Then in mockery they dressed him in an old purple robe, saying: "It is fitting for our new king to be clothed and crowned." So they gathered thorns and made a crown like the crowns of gold and precious stones that kings wear on their heads, and they set this crown of thorns on Judas's head, putting a reed in his hand for a sceptre, and made him sit in a high place. And the soldiers came before him, bowing in mockery and hailing him king of the Jews; and they held out their hands to receive gifts, as new kings are in the habit of giving — and getting nothing, they beat Judas, saying: "Now how are you crowned, you foolish king, if you will not pay your soldiers and your servants!"

The chief priests with the scribes and Pharisees, seeing that Judas did not die under the whips and fearing that Pilate might set him free, gave money to the governor; and he, having taken it, handed Judas over to the scribes and Pharisees as guilty and deserving death. Then with him they condemned two robbers to death on the cross. So they led him to Mount Calvary, where wrongdoers were hanged, and there they crucified him naked, for the greater shame.

Judas truly did nothing but cry out: "God, why have you forsaken me — the evildoer is fled, and I am wrongfully put to death!" I tell you in truth, the voice and the face, together with the whole person, of Judas were so like to Jesus that his disciples and all who believed wholly thought he was Jesus. So some fell away from the teaching of Jesus, believing that Jesus was a false prophet and that by magic art he had done the miracles he did — for Jesus had said that he would not die until near the end of the world, since at that time he would have been taken up from the world. But those who stood firm in the teaching of Jesus were so ringed round with sorrow, seeing him die who was in all things like to Jesus, that they could not remember what Jesus had said. So in company with the mother of Jesus they went to Mount Calvary, and stood watching the death of Judas, weeping all the while; and through Nicodemus and Joseph of Arimathea they got the governor to give them the body of Judas for burial. So they took him down from the cross with such weeping as no one would surely believe, and buried him in Joseph's new tomb, having wrapped him with a hundred pounds of precious ointment.

## Chapter 218


Everyone went back home. He who writes, with John and James his brother, went with the mother of Jesus to Nazareth. Those disciples who did not fear God went by night and stole the body of Judas and hid it, spreading the report that Jesus was risen again — so great confusion arose. Then the high priest commanded, under pain of anathema, that no one speak of Jesus of Nazareth; so a great persecution arose, and many were stoned, many beaten, and many driven from their homeland, because they could not keep silent about such a thing. The news reached Nazareth that Jesus, their fellow-citizen, having died on the cross, was risen again. So he who writes begged the mother of Jesus to be content to leave off weeping, because her son was risen again. Hearing this, the virgin Mary, weeping, said: "Let us go to Jerusalem to find my son, for I shall die content when I have seen him."

## Chapter 219


The virgin returned to Jerusalem with him who writes and James and John, on the day on which the decree of the high priest went forth. But the virgin, who feared God, although she knew the high priest's decree was unjust, commanded those who dwelled with her to forget her son. Now how each one was affected — God, who knows the heart of men, knows: between grief at the death of Judas, whom we believed to be Jesus our master, and the desire to see him risen again, we were consumed, with the mother of Jesus. Then the angels who were guardians of Mary the virgin ascended to the third heaven, where Jesus was in the company of angels, and recounted everything to him. So Jesus prayed God to give him power to see his mother and his disciples. Then the merciful God commanded his four favourite angels — Gabriel, Michael, Rafael, and Uriel — to carry Jesus into his mother's house, and there to keep watch over him for three days continually, letting him be seen only by those who believed in his doctrine. Jesus came, surrounded with splendour, into the room where Mary the virgin abode with her two sisters, and Martha with Mary Magdalen, and Lazarus with him who writes and John with James and Peter. And through fear they fell as dead; but Jesus lifted his mother and the others from the ground, saying: "Do not fear, for I am Jesus; and do not weep, for I am alive and not dead." Everyone remained for a long time beside himself at the presence of Jesus, for they altogether believed that Jesus was dead. Then the virgin, weeping, said: "Tell me, my son: why, when God gave you power to raise the dead, did you let yourself die so, to the shame of your kinsfolk and friends and to the shame of your doctrine? For everyone who loves you has been as dead."

## Chapter 220


Jesus answered, embracing his mother: "Believe me, mother, for in truth I tell you that I have never died at all; for God has reserved me until near the end of the world." And having said this, he prayed the four angels to uncover themselves and to give testimony how the matter had passed. So the angels uncovered themselves like four shining suns, so that through fear everyone fell again as dead. Then Jesus gave four linen cloths to the angels, so that they might cover themselves, that they might be seen and heard speaking. He lifted up his mother with her companions, and having lifted everyone up, he comforted them, saying: "These are the ministers of God: Gabriel, who announces God's secrets; Michael, who fights against God's enemies; Rafael, who receives the souls of those who die; and Uriel, who will call everyone to God's judgement on the last day." Then the four angels narrated to the virgin how God had sent for Jesus, and how he had transformed Judas, that he might suffer the punishment which he had sold to another. Then said he who writes: "O master, is it lawful for me to question you now, as it was lawful for me when you dwelled with us?" Jesus answered: "Ask what pleases you, Barnabas, and I will answer you." Then said he who writes: "O master, since God is merciful, why has he so tormented us, making us believe that you were dead? And your mother has so wept for you that she has been near to death; and you, who are a holy one of God — has God let this infamy fall upon you, that you were slain among robbers on Mount Calvary?" Jesus answered: "Believe me, Barnabas, that every sin, however small it be, God punishes with great punishment, since God is offended by sin. So since my mother and the faithful disciples who were with me loved me a little with earthly love, the just God has willed to punish this love with the present grief, so that it may not be punished in the flames of hell. And as for me, who have been innocent in the world — since men have called me God and Son of God — God, so as not to let me be mocked by the demons on the day of judgement, has willed that I be mocked by men in the world through the death of Judas, making everyone believe that I died on the cross. And this mocking will last until the coming of Mohammed, messenger of God,* who, coming to the world, will undeceive everyone who believes in the law of God from this deception." Having said this, Jesus said: "You are just, Lord our God, for to you alone belong honour and glory without end."

\* **The seam passage, translated exactly as written**: *onde quessto scerno durera inssino alia uenuta di machometo nontio di DIOC . il quale uenendo al modo sganera ogniuno che chrederano alia legie di DIO di quessto ingano* — "and this mocking will last until the coming of Mohammed, messenger of God, who, coming to the world, will undeceive everyone who believes in the law of God from this deception." Nothing is smoothed, softened, or deleted. The fork is footnoted per the audit: Robert's recorded suspicion that the named-Muhammad line may be a later insertion is carried here rather than decided; the naming of Ahmad/Muhammad is itself Quranic (61:6), and the sharper Quran friction in this lane remains the passages where Jesus denies being the Messiah, against Quran 3:45 ("the Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary") — never decided here. (See SOURCE-AUDIT §3.)

## Chapter 221


And Jesus, turning to him who writes, said: "See, Barnabas, that by all means you write my gospel concerning all that has happened through my dwelling in the world; and write likewise what has befallen Judas, so that the faithful may be undeceived and everyone may believe the truth." He who writes answered: "All will I do, God willing, O master; but how it happened to Judas I do not know, for I did not see it all." Jesus answered: "Here are John and Peter, who have seen it all, and they will tell you how it all passed." And then Jesus commanded that we should call his faithful disciples, that they might see him. So James and John gathered together the seven disciples with Nicodemus and Joseph and many others of the seventy-two, and they ate with Jesus. On the third day Jesus said: "Go to the Mount of Olives with my mother, for there I will ascend again to heaven, and you will see who will bear me up." So everyone went, except twenty-five of the seventy-two disciples, who for fear had fled to Damascus. And as everyone stood in prayer, at midday Jesus came with a great multitude of angels who were praising God; and from the splendour of his face everyone was sore afraid, and they fell with their faces to the ground. Then Jesus, lifting them up, comforted them, saying: "Do not fear, for I am your master." And he reproved many who believed him to have died and risen again, saying: "Do you then hold me and God for a liar? For God has granted to me to live until near the end of the world, even as I told you. And I tell you that I did not die, but Judas the traitor died. Beware, for Satan will make every effort to deceive you; so see to it that you be my witnesses in all Israel and throughout the world of all you have heard and seen." And having said this, he prayed God for the salvation of the faithful and for the conversion of sinners. When the prayer was ended, he embraced his mother, saying: "Peace be to you, my mother; rest in God your creator and mine." And having said this, he turned to the disciples, saying: "May God's grace and mercy be with you." And having said this, the four angels visibly lifted him up to heaven.

## Chapter 222


After Jesus had departed, the disciples scattered through the different parts of Israel and of the world; and the truth, hated by Satan, was persecuted by falsehood, as it still is. For certain evil men, pretending to be disciples, preached that Jesus died and rose not again. Others preached that he really died, but rose again. Others preached, and still preach, that Jesus is the Son of God — among whom is Paul, deceived. But we, as much as I have written, preach to those who fear God, that they may be saved in the last day of God's judgement. Amen.

End of the gospel.
